Essentially exact numerical modelling of flux qubit chains subject to charge and flux noise Preprint: APS/123-QED
Matthew R.C. Fitzpatrick, Jack Raymond, Malcolm P. Kennett

Abstract
We present an essentially exact numerical method for modelling flux qubit chains subject to charge and flux noise. We define an essentially exact method as one that introduces errors that are completely controlled such that they can be made arbitrarily small by tuning the simulation parameters. The method adopts the quasi-adiabatic path integral formalism to express the system’s reduced density matrix as a time-discretized path integral, comprising a series of influence functionals that encode the non-Markovian dynamics of the system. We present a detailed derivation of the path integral expression for the system’s reduced density matrix and describe in detail the tensor network algorithm used to evaluate the path integral expression. We have implemented our method in an open-sourced Python library called spinbosonchain ( D-Wave Systems 2021 ) . When appropriate, we draw connections between concepts covered in this manuscript and the library’s code.



I
Introduction

Quantum annealing has proven to be an effective approach to a variety
of optimization problems. However, outside the formalism of adiabatic
quantum computing, characterizing and predicting the performance of
quantum annealing has proven to be a great challenge. Accurate modelling
of environmental noise in quantum annealing is therefore of critical
importance to the design of future architectures. One of the primary
sources of noise in D-Wave quantum annealers is flux noise, which
can be decomposed into 1/f-like and ohmic-like components. For fast
anneals on the order of tens of nanoseconds, charge noise also becomes
appreciable. Recently, a method has been developed for simulating
flux qubit chains that are subject to generic flux noise
(
Strathearn et al. 2018
;
Suzuki et al. 2019
;
Oshiyama et al. 2020
)
.
By combining the quasi-adiabatic path integral (QUAPI) formalism with
tensor network (TN) techniques, the method is computationally efficient,
essentially exact, and captures non-Markovian effects of the flux
noise. We define an essentially exact method as one that introduces
errors that are completely controlled such that they can be made arbitrarily
small by tuning the simulation parameters
1
1

1

The computational resources required to achieve a particular target
error will depend on the system being studied. In some scenarios,
e.g. systems dominated by strong low-frequency noise, the target error
may scale poorly with computer memory and/or wall time, especially
if one is interested in long-time dynamics.
. While the QUAPI+TN method accurately models flux noise in D-Wave
devices, it does not capture the effects of charge noise.

In this manuscript, we expand on the works of Refs.
(
Strathearn et al. 2018
;
Suzuki et al. 2019
;
Oshiyama et al. 2020
)

by extending the QUAPI approach to include the effects of charge and
flux noise within the same formalism, and to modify the tensor network
algorithm accordingly. We have implemented this generalized approach
in a Python library called
spinbosonchain

(
D-Wave Systems 2021
)
,
which makes heavy use of Google’s
TensorNetwork
package

(
Ten 2019
)
for its implementation of tensor networks
and related operations. This manuscript describes in detail the generalized
QUAPI formalism as well as the tensor network algorithms on which

spinbosonchain
is based. When appropriate, we draw connections
between some of the concepts covered in this manuscript to the library’s
code. This manuscript does not describe in detail the application
programming interface of
spinbosonchain
: readers interested
in this can consult the
spinbosonchain
reference guide

(
D-Wave Systems 2021
)
.

This manuscript is structured as follows: In Sec.
II
,
we suggest how those interested in reviewing the code of the
spinbosonchain

library should do so. In Sec.
III
we introduce
our extended QUAPI formalism used to describe the dynamics of a generalized
one-dimensional spin-boson chain model, where both the
z
- and
y
-components
of the quantum Ising spins are coupled to bosonic baths, rather than
only the
z
-components. The main result of this section is our time-discretized
path integral expression of the system’s reduced state operator. In
Sec.
IV
, we develop the tensor network
algorithm used to evaluate the path integral, and discuss what quantities
can be calculated using our approach. Finally, in Sec.
V
,
we make some remarks about our method and present our conclusions.

III
QUAPI formalism

III.1
Model

spinbosonchain
is a library for simulating the dynamics
of a generalized one-dimensional spin-boson chain model, where both
the
z
- and
y
-components of the quantum Ising spins are coupled
to bosonic baths, rather than only the
z
-components. A convenient
way to discuss both finite and infinite chains is to express the Hamiltonian
of the aforementioned spin-boson model as a sum of 2
N
+1 ‘unit
cell’ Hamiltonians:

\hat{H}\left(t\right)\equiv\sum_{u=-N}^{N}\hat{H}_{u}\left(t\right),

(1)

where
N
is a non-negative integer, and
\hat{H}_{u}\left(t\right)

is the Hamiltonian of the
u^{\text{th}}
unit cell of the model:

\hat{H}_{u}\left(t\right)=\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)+\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}+\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(AB\right)}\left(t\right),

(2)

with
\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)
being
the system part of
\hat{H}_{u}\left(t\right)
, which encodes all
information regarding energies associated exclusively with the spins;

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}
being the bath part of
\hat{H}_{u}\left(t\right)
,
which encodes all information regarding energies associated with the
components of the bosonic environment; and
\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(AB\right)}\left(t\right)

is the system-bath coupling part of
\hat{H}_{u}\left(t\right)
,
which describes all energies associated with the coupling between
the system and the environment.

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)
is simply an instance
of the one-dimensional transverse-field Ising model:

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)=\hat{H}_{u;x}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)+\hat{H}_{u;z}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right),

(3)

\hat{H}_{u;x}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)\equiv\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}h_{x;r}\left(t\right)\hat{\sigma}_{x;r+uL},

(4)

\hat{H}_{u;z}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)\equiv\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}h_{z;r}\left(t\right)\hat{\sigma}_{z;r+uL}+\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}J_{z,z;r,r+1}\left(t\right)\hat{\sigma}_{z;r+uL}\hat{\sigma}_{z;r+1+uL},

(5)

with
\hat{\sigma}_{\nu;r}
being the
\nu^{\text{th}}

Pauli operator acting on site
r
,
h_{x;r}\left(t\right)
being
the transverse field energy scale for site
r
at time
t
,
h_{z;r}\left(t\right)

being the longitudinal field energy scale for site
r
at time
t
,

J_{z,z;r,r+1}\left(t\right)
being the longitudinal coupling energy
scale for sites
r
and
r+1
at time
t
, and
L
being the number
of sites in every unit cell.

In
spinbosonchain
, the system’s model parameters
h_{x;r}\left(t\right)
,

h_{z;r}\left(t\right)
, and
J_{z,z;r,r+1}\left(t\right)
are represented
by

spinbosonchain.scalar.Scalar
objects. Generally speaking,
the
spinbosonchain.scalar.Scalar
class represents time-dependent
scalar model parameters. The system’s model parameter set is represented
by the

spinbosonchain.system.Model
class.

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}
describes a collection of
decoupled harmonic oscillators:

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}=\sum_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\}}\hat{H}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)},

(6)

\hat{H}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)}=\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\sum_{\epsilon}\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}},

(7)

with
\epsilon
being the oscillator mode index,
\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\dagger}

and
\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}
being the bosonic
creation and annihilation operators respectively for the harmonic
oscillator at site
r
in mode
\epsilon
with angular frequency

\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}>0
coupled to the
\nu^{\text{th}}
component
of the spin at the same site.

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(AB\right)}\left(t\right)
is given
by

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(AB\right)}\left(t\right)=\hat{H}_{u;y}^{\left(AB\right)}\left(t\right)+\hat{H}_{u;z}^{\left(AB\right)}\left(t\right),

(8)

\hat{H}_{u;\nu}^{\left(AB\right)}\left(t\right)=-\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\hat{\sigma}_{\nu;r+uL}\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\nu;r+uL}\left(t\right),

(9)

where
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\nu;r}\left(t\right)
is the
generalized reservoir force operator at site
r
that acts on the

\nu^{\text{th}}
component of the spin at the same site:

\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\nu;r+uL}\left(t\right)=\mathcal{E}_{v;r}^{\left(\lambda\right)}\left(t\right)\hat{Q}_{\nu;r+uL},

(10)

with
\mathcal{E}_{v;r}^{\left(\lambda\right)}\left(t\right)

being a time-dependent energy scale,
\hat{Q}_{\nu;r}
being a rescaled
generalized reservoir force:

\hat{Q}_{\nu;r+uL}=-\sum_{\epsilon}\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}\left\{\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\dagger}+\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}\right\},

(11)

with
\lambda_{\nu;\epsilon}
being the coupling strength
between the
\nu^{\text{th}}
component of the spin at site
r

and the harmonic oscillator at the same site in mode
\epsilon
.

Note that the bosonic creation and annihilations operators satisfy
the following commutation relation:

\left[\hat{b}_{\nu_{1};r_{1};\epsilon_{1}}^{\vphantom{\dagger}},\hat{b}_{\nu_{2};r_{2};\epsilon_{2}}^{\dagger}\right]=\delta_{\nu_{1},\nu_{2}}\delta_{r_{1},r_{2}}\delta_{\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}}\hat{1}_{\nu_{1};r_{1};\epsilon_{1}}^{\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}\hat{1}_{\nu_{2};r_{2};\epsilon_{2}}^{\left(\mathcal{B}\right)},

(12)

where
\hat{1}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}

is the identity operator of the Hilbert space associated with the
harmonic oscillator at site
r
in mode
\epsilon
, coupled to the

\nu^{\text{th}}
component of the spin at the same site, and
\delta_{i,j}

is the Kronecker delta function.

For finite chains, we set
N=0
[see Eq. (
1
)]
and
J_{z,z;L-1,L}\left(t\right)=0
[see Eq. (
5
)],
whereas for infinite chains, we take the limit of
N\to\infty
.

III.2
Initial state of system

We assume that the spin system and bath together are initially prepared
in a state at time
t=0
corresponding to the state operator:

\hat{\rho}^{\left(i\right)}\equiv\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\otimes\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,B\right)},

(13)

where
\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}
is the system’s reduced
state operator at
t=0
:

\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\equiv\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right|,

(14)

with

\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle\equiv\bigotimes_{u=-N}^{N}\left|\Psi_{u}^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle,

(15)

and
\left|\Psi_{u}^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle
being
a state vector describing the reduced state of the
u^{\text{th}}

unit cell;
\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,B\right)}
is the bath’s reduced
state operator at
t=0
:

\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,B\right)}\equiv\bigotimes_{u=-N}^{N}\hat{\rho}_{u}^{\left(i,B\right)},

(16)

\hat{\rho}_{u}^{\left(i,B\right)}\equiv\frac{e^{-\beta\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}}}{\mathcal{Z}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}},

(17)

where
\beta=1/\left(k_{B}T\right)
,
k_{B}
is the Boltzmann
constant,
T
is the temperature,
\mathcal{Z}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}

is the partition function of the bath:

\mathcal{Z}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}=\text{Tr}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{e^{-\beta\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}}\right\},

(18)

and
\text{Tr}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\cdots\right\}
is the partial
trace with respect to the bath degrees of freedom. Using Eq. (
12
),
we can write:

\hat{\rho}_{u}^{\left(i,B\right)}=\hat{\rho}_{u;y}^{\left(i,B\right)}\hat{\rho}_{u;z}^{\left(i,B\right)},

(19)

\hat{\rho}_{u;\nu}^{\left(i,B\right)}=\frac{e^{-\beta\hat{H}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)}}}{\mathcal{Z}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)}},

(20)

where

\mathcal{Z}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)}=\text{Tr}_{\nu}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{e^{-\beta\hat{H}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)}}\right\},

(21)

and
\text{Tr}_{\nu}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\cdots\right\}

is the partial trace with respect to the bath degrees of freedom coupled
to the
\nu^{\text{th}}
components of the spins. The trace
\text{Tr}_{\nu}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\cdots\right\}

can be evaluated using the bosonic occupation number basis states

\left|n_{\nu;r;\epsilon}\right\rangle
, where

\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}\left|n_{\nu^{\prime};r^{\prime};\epsilon^{\prime}}\right\rangle=\begin{cases}n_{\nu^{\prime};r^{\prime};\epsilon^{\prime}}\left|n_{\nu^{\prime};r^{\prime};\epsilon^{\prime}}\right\rangle,&\text{if }\nu=\nu^{\prime}\text{ \& }r=r^{\prime}\text{ \& }\epsilon=\epsilon^{\prime},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(22)

Using Eqs. (
7
), and
(
22
), we can evaluate
Eq. (
21
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)}

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{\nu}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{e^{-\beta\hat{H}_{u;\nu}^{\left(B\right)}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\text{Tr}_{\nu}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{\epsilon}\left[e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{\epsilon}\left[\sum_{n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}=0}^{\infty}\right]\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{\epsilon}\left[\left\langle n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}\right|e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}}\left|n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{\epsilon}\left[\sum_{n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}=0}^{\infty}\left\langle n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}\right|e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\dagger}\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}}\left|n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}\right\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{\epsilon}\left[\sum_{n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{\epsilon}\left[\sum_{n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}=0}^{\infty}\left(e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right)^{n_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}}\right]

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{\epsilon}\left[\frac{1}{\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}}\right],

(23)

where in the last step we used the geometric series identity

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a^{n}=\frac{1}{1-a},\quad\text{for }0<\left|a\right|<1

(24)

and we introduced the
\Pi
product notation. When limits
are present explicitly in the
\Pi
product notation, the following
convention is adopted:

\prod_{l=l_{i}}^{l_{f}}\left\{x_{l_{\vphantom{i}}}\right\}=\begin{cases}x_{l_{i}}x_{l_{i}+1}\cdots x_{l_{f}-1}x_{l_{f}},&\text{if }l_{f}\geq l_{i},\\
1,&\text{if }l_{f}<l_{i}.\end{cases}

(25)

III.3
Evolution operator

The evolution operator
\hat{U}\left(t,t^{\prime}\right)
can be
expressed as
(
Stefanucci and van
Leeuwen 2013
)
:

\displaystyle\hat{U}\left(t,t^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}T\left\{e^{-i\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}dt^{\prime\prime}\hat{H}\left(t^{\prime\prime}\right)}\right\},&\text{if }t\geq t^{\prime},\\
\tilde{T}\left\{e^{i\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\prime\prime}\hat{H}\left(t^{\prime\prime}\right)}\right\},&\text{if }t<t^{\prime},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\equiv\begin{cases}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-i\right)^{n}}{n!}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\left\{\int_{t^{\prime}}^{t}dt^{\left(m\right)}\right\}T\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{n}\left[\hat{H}\left(t^{\left(m\right)}\right)\right]\right\},&\text{if }t\geq t^{\prime},\\
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(i\right)^{n}}{n!}\prod_{m=1}^{n}\left\{\int_{t}^{t^{\prime}}dt^{\left(m\right)}\right\}\tilde{T}\left\{\prod_{m=1}^{n}\left[\hat{H}\left(t^{\left(m\right)}\right)\right]\right\},&\text{if }t<t^{\prime},\end{cases}

(26)

where
\hat{H}\left(t\right)
is the total Hamiltonian
[see Eq. (
1
)]
T\left\{\cdots\right\}

is the time ordering symbol, which specifies that the string of time-dependent
operators contained within
T\left\{\cdots\right\}
be rearranged
as a time-descending sequence, and
\tilde{T}\left\{\cdots\right\}

is the anti-time ordering symbol, which orders strings of time-dependent
operators in the reverse order with respect to
T\left\{\cdots\right\}
.
For example, in the case of three operators, we have for
T\left\{\cdots\right\}

T\left\{\hat{A}\left(t^{\left(1\right)}\right)\hat{B}\left(t^{\left(2\right)}\right)\hat{C}\left(t^{\left(3\right)}\right)\right\}=\begin{cases}\hat{A}\left(t^{\left(1\right)}\right)\hat{B}\left(t^{\left(2\right)}\right)\hat{C}\left(t^{\left(3\right)}\right),&\text{if }t^{\left(1\right)}>t^{\left(2\right)}>t^{\left(3\right)},\\
\hat{A}\left(t^{\left(1\right)}\right)\hat{C}\left(t^{\left(3\right)}\right)\hat{B}\left(t^{\left(2\right)}\right),&\text{if }t^{\left(1\right)}>t^{\left(3\right)}>t^{\left(2\right)},\\
\hat{B}\left(t^{\left(2\right)}\right)\hat{A}\left(t^{\left(1\right)}\right)\hat{C}\left(t^{\left(3\right)}\right),&\text{if }t^{\left(2\right)}>t^{\left(1\right)}>t^{\left(3\right)},\\
\hat{B}\left(t^{\left(2\right)}\right)\hat{C}\left(t^{\left(3\right)}\right)\hat{A}\left(t^{\left(1\right)}\right),&\text{if }t^{\left(2\right)}>t^{\left(3\right)}>t^{\left(1\right)},\\
\hat{C}\left(t^{\left(3\right)}\right)\hat{A}\left(t^{\left(1\right)}\right)\hat{B}\left(t^{\left(2\right)}\right),&\text{if }t^{\left(3\right)}>t^{\left(1\right)}>t^{\left(2\right)},\\
\hat{C}\left(t^{\left(3\right)}\right)\hat{B}\left(t^{\left(2\right)}\right)\hat{A}\left(t^{\left(1\right)}\right),&\text{if }t^{\left(3\right)}>t^{\left(2\right)}>t^{\left(1\right)}.\end{cases}

(27)

One can show by differentiating both sides of Eq. (
26
)
with respect to
t
that
\hat{U}\left(t,t^{\prime}\right)
satisfies
the following equations of motion
(
Stefanucci and van
Leeuwen 2013
)
:

\frac{\partial\hat{U}\left(t,t^{\prime}\right)}{\partial t}=-i\hat{H}\left(t\right)\hat{U}\left(t,t^{\prime}\right),

(28)

\frac{\partial\hat{U}\left(t^{\prime},t\right)}{\partial t}=i\hat{U}\left(t^{\prime},t\right)\hat{H}\left(t\right).

(29)

III.4
Spectral densities of noise

Rather than specify the bath model parameters
\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}

and
\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}
[introduced in Eqs. (
7
)
and (
11
)], one can alternatively
specify the spectral densities of noise coupled to the
y
- and
z
-components
of the system’s spins at temperature
T
, which contain
the same information as the aforementioned model parameters. This
alternative approach is easier to incorporate into the QUAPI formalism,
upon which
spinbosonchain
is based on.

We define the spectral density of the noise coupled to the
\nu
-component
of the spin at site
r
and temperature
T
as:

A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}dt\,e^{i\omega t}C_{\nu;r;T}\left(t\right),

(30)

where
C_{\nu;r;T}\left(t\right)
is the bath correlation
function of the noise coupled to the
\nu
-component of the spin
at site
r
and temperature
T
:

C_{\nu;r;T}\left(t\right)=\text{Tr}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,B\right)}\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\nu;r}^{\left(B\right)}\left(t\right)\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\nu;r}^{\left(B\right)}\left(0\right)\right\},

(31)

with
\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\nu;r}^{\left(B\right)}\left(t\right)

being the rescaled reservoir force operator in the Heisenberg picture:

\hat{\mathcal{Q}}_{\nu;r}^{\left(B\right)}\left(t\right)=e^{i\hat{H}^{\left(B\right)}t}\hat{Q}_{\nu;r}e^{-i\hat{H}^{\left(B\right)}t},

(32)

\hat{Q}_{\nu;r}
being given by Eq. (
11
),

\hat{H}^{\left(B\right)}=\sum_{u=-N}^{N}\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)},

(33)

\hat{H}_{u}^{\left(B\right)}
being given by Eq. (
6
),
and
\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,B\right)}
being given by Eq. (
16
).
In other words,
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
is the Fourier transform
of
C_{\nu;r;T}\left(t\right)
.

Let us calculate
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
for the case of
the generalized spin-boson model defined in Sec. (
III.1
).
First, we calculate
C_{\nu;r;T}\left(t\right)
using Eqs. (
6
),
(
7
), (
10
)-(
12
),
(
16
), (
19
), (
20
),
(
22
), (
23
),
(
31
), (
32
):

C_{\nu;r;T}\left(t\right)=\sum_{\epsilon}\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}\left[ne^{i\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}t}+\left\{n+1\right\}e^{-i\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}t}\right].

(34)

Next, using Eqs. (
24
),
(
30
),
and (
34
),
we can evaluate
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
as follows:

\displaystyle A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}\left[n\delta\left(\omega+\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{=}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}}\left.\quad+\left\{n+1\right\}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega+\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}n

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}n

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega+\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\left\{-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\left\{-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\left[\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\left\{\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}n}\right\}

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega+\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\left\{-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\left[\frac{1}{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\left\{-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{\partial}{\partial\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\left[\frac{1}{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}\left\{\frac{1}{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}}\right\}

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega+\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\cancel{\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}}\left\{-\frac{1}{\cancel{\beta}}\left[-\frac{\left(\cancel{\beta}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right)}{\left(1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right)^{\cancel{2}}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\cancel{\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}}\left\{-\frac{1}{\cancel{\beta}}\left[-\frac{\left(\cancel{\beta}e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right)}{\left(1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right)^{\cancel{2}}}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\cancel{\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}\right\}}\left\{\frac{1}{\cancel{1-e^{-\beta\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}}}}\right\}

\displaystyle\text{*Use: }\delta\left(\omega\pm\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)f\left(\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)=\delta\left(\omega\pm\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)f\left(\mp\omega\right),

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega+\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\frac{e^{\beta\omega}}{1-e^{\beta\omega}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\frac{e^{-\beta\omega}}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{+}2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}

\displaystyle=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\frac{\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}}\left[\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)-\delta\left(\omega+\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right)\right].

(35)

Note that the zero-temperature limit of
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)

yields:

A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)=\lim_{T\to 0}A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=2\pi\sum_{\epsilon}\lambda_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{2}\delta\left(\omega-\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}\right).

(36)

Lastly, using Eqs. (
35
)
and (
36
),
we get

A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}},

(37)

where

\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)=\begin{cases}1&\text{if }\omega>0,\\
-1&\text{if }\omega<0.\end{cases}

(38)

In many applications of interest,
A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)

will comprise of multiple components:

A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)=\sum_{\varsigma}A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right),

(39)

where
A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)
is the

\varsigma^{\text{th}}
component of
A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)
.
As an example,
A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)
may be strongly
peaked at multiple frequencies, in which case it naturally decomposes
into multiple components. If
A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)
naturally
decomposes into multiple components, it is best to treat each component
separately in our QUAPI approach rather than treat
A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)

as a single entity.

From Eqs. (
37
)
and (
39
),
we can write

A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=\sum_{\varsigma}A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right),

(40)

where

A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)=\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}}.

(41)

In the continuum limit,
A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)

becomes a continuous function of
\omega
satisfying

A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)\geq 0,

(42)

and

A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\leq 0\right)=0.

(43)

In order for the noisy dynamics to be “well-behaved”,

A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)
must also satisfy

\left|\lim_{\omega\to 0^{+}}\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)}{\omega}\right|<\infty.

(44)

For a given source of noise, described by the spectral density of
noise
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
at temperature
T
, we can
characterize the strength of this noise by calculating:

W_{\nu;r;T}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right).

(45)

In
spinbosonchain
, the quantities
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
,

A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)
,
A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)
,
and
A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)
are represented
by the classes
spinbosonchain.bath.SpectralDensity
,
spinbosonchain.bath.SpectralDensity0T
,

spinbosonchain.bath.SpectralDensityCmpnt
, and
spinbosonchain.bath.SpectralDensityCmpnt0T

respectively. For a discussion on how these quantities are evaluated
numerically in
spinbosonchain
, see Appendix
C
.
The quantities
C_{\nu;r;T}\left(t\right)
and
W_{\nu;r;T}
can
be calculated using the functions
spinbosonchain.bath.correlation

and
spinbosonchain.bath.noise_strength
respectively.

III.5
Reduced state operator of the device

The full state operator at time
t
,
\hat{\rho}\left(t\right)
,
can be expressed as

\hat{\rho}\left(t\right)=\hat{U}\left(t,0\right)\hat{\rho}^{\left(i\right)}\hat{U}\left(0,t\right),

(46)

where
\hat{\rho}^{\left(i\right)}
and
\hat{U}\left(t,0\right)

are given by Eqs. (
13
) and (
26
)
respectively. We are ultimately interested in calculating the reduced
state operator of the device:

\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)=\text{Tr}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}\left(t\right)\right\}.

(47)

To calculate
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)
efficiently
using tensor network methods, we discretize time over a uniform grid
of step size
\Delta t
and express the reduced state operator in
path integral form. There are various ways to express
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

as a path integral. Here we loosely draw from Refs.
(
Makri and Makarov 1995a
;
Makri and Makarov 1995b
;
Strathearn et al. 2018
;
Suzuki et al. 2019
;
Oshiyama et al. 2020
)
,
wherein a single particle or spin is considered. In Appendix
B
,
we derive the following path integral representation of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

that is accurate to second order in the time step size
\Delta t
:

\displaystyle\rho^{\left(A\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;n+1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;n+1}\right)

\displaystyle\equiv\left\langle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;n+1}z\right|\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\left|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;n+1}z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{\alpha=\pm 1}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left[\prod_{q=0}^{q_{y;n}}\left\{\sum_{\sigma_{y;r+uL;\alpha;q}=\pm 1}\right\}\prod_{q=0}^{q_{z;n}-1}\left\{\sum_{\sigma_{z;r+uL;\alpha;q}=\pm 1}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\mathop{\times}e^{i\sum_{\alpha=\pm 1}\phi_{\alpha;n}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right)}I_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\mathop{\times}\rho^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;0},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;0}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left[\Delta t^{2}\right],

(48)

where
2N+1
is the number of unit cells; “lcafc” is
an initialism for “longitudinal coupler and field components”;

n\geq 1
;

q_{y;n}=\begin{cases}-1,&\text{if }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
2n+1,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(49)

with
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
being the spectral
density of the noise coupled to the
\nu
-component of the spin at
site
r
and temperature
T
[given by Eq. (
30
)],

q_{z;n}=n+1;

(50)

\hat{\sigma}_{\nu;r}\left|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\nu;\alpha;q}\nu\right\rangle=\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q}\left|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\nu;\alpha;q}\nu\right\rangle,

(51)

with

\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\nu;\alpha;q}=\left(\sigma_{\nu;r=-NL;\alpha;q},\ldots,\sigma_{\nu;r=NL+L-1;\alpha;q}\right),

(52)

\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\}
,
\hat{\sigma}_{\nu;r}
being
the
\nu^{\text{th}}
Pauli operator acting on site
r
,
\alpha=\pm 1
,
and
q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]
;

\rho^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;0},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;0}\right)=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;0}z\vphantom{\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}}\right.\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right|\left.\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;0}z\vphantom{\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}}\right\rangle,

(53)

with
\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}=\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right|

being the system’s reduced state operator at the initial time,
t=0
;

\phi_{\alpha;n}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right)=\sum_{q=0}^{q_{z;n}}\phi_{\alpha;n;k=q}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q}\right),

(54)

with

\phi_{\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q}\right)=-\frac{\alpha\Delta t}{2}\sum_{k^{\prime}=k-1}^{k}w_{n;k^{\prime}}H_{z}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{k^{\prime}};\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q}\right),

(55)

H_{z}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{k^{\prime}};\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q}\right)=\sum_{u=-N}^{N}H_{u;z}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{k^{\prime}};\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q}\right),

(56)

H_{u;z}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t;\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q}\right)=\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}h_{z;r}\left(t\right)\sigma_{z;r+uL;\alpha;q}+\sum_{r=1}^{L-1}J_{z,z;r,r+1}\left(t\right)\sigma_{z;r+uL;\alpha;q}\sigma_{z;r+uL+1;\alpha;q},

(57)

w_{n;k}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }k=-1,n+1,\\
\frac{1}{2},&\text{if }k=0,n,\\
1,&\text{if }0<k<n,\end{cases}

(58)

h_{z;r}\left(t\right)
being the longitudinal field energy
scale for site
r
at time
t
,
J_{z,z;r,r+1}\left(t\right)
being
the longitudinal coupling energy scale for sites
r
and
r+1
at
time
t
, and
L
being the size of the unit cell;

I_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{I_{r+uL;n}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r+uL;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)\right\},

(59)

with
I_{r;n}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)

being the influence functional that couples the Ising spin variables

\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}

with one another [including Ising spin variables associated with
different times]:

I_{r;n}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)=\begin{cases}I_{r;n}^{\left(\text{z-noise}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right),&\text{if }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
I_{r;n}^{\left(\text{yz-noise}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right),&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(60)

\displaystyle I_{r;n}^{\left(\text{yz-noise}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle=I_{n}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}I_{y;r;n}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{y;n}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\}}\left\{I_{\nu;r;n}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)\right\},

(61)

I_{r;n}^{\left(\text{z-noise}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right)=I_{z;r;n}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right)I_{z;r;n}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right),

(62)

\displaystyle I_{n}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{l=0}^{n}\left\{I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1,2l+1},\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l+1},\sigma_{z;r;1,l+1},\sigma_{z;r;-1,l+1}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{=\prod_{l=0}^{n}}\left.\quad\mathop{\times}I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1,2l},\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l},\sigma_{z;r;1,l},\sigma_{z;r;-1,l}\right)\right\},

(63)

\displaystyle I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1,2l+1},\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l+1},\sigma_{z;r;1,l+1},\sigma_{z;r;-1,l+1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=I^{\left(y\to z\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;1,l+1},\sigma_{y;r;1,2l+1}\right)I^{\left(z\to y\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l+1},\sigma_{z;r;-1,l+1}\right),

(64)

I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1,2l},\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l},\sigma_{z;r;1,l},\sigma_{z;r;-1,l}\right)=I^{\left(z\to y\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1,2l},\sigma_{z;r;1,l}\right)I^{\left(y\to z\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;-1,l},\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l}\right),

(65)

\displaystyle I^{\left(z\to y\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q^{\vphantom{\prime}}},\sigma_{z;r;\alpha;q^{\prime}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{2}}\left\{1-i\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q^{\vphantom{\prime}}}+\sigma_{z;r;\alpha;q^{\prime}}+i\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q^{\vphantom{\prime}}}\sigma_{z;r;\alpha;q^{\prime}}\right\},

(66)

\displaystyle I^{\left(y\to z\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha;q^{\prime}},\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q^{\vphantom{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{I^{\left(z\to y\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q^{\vphantom{\prime}}},\sigma_{z;r;\alpha;q^{\prime}}\right)\right\}^{*},

(67)

I_{y;r;n}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{y;n}\right]}\right)=\prod_{l=0}^{n}\left\{I_{y;r;n;k=l}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1;2l},\sigma_{y;r;-1;2l},\sigma_{y;r;1;2l+1},\sigma_{y;r;-1;2l+1}\right)\right\},

(68)

I_{z;r;n}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right)=\prod_{l=0}^{n}\left\{I_{z;r;n;k=l}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;1;l},\sigma_{z;r;-1;l},\sigma_{z;r;1;l+1},\sigma_{z;r;-1;l+1}\right)\right\},

(69)

\displaystyle I_{\nu;r;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=I_{\nu;r;1;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}}\right)I_{\nu;r;-1;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{2}}\right),

(70)

\displaystyle I_{\nu;r;\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q_{1}},\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{1}}+\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{2}}\right)^{2}\cos\left(\frac{\theta_{r;n;k}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\mathop{+}i^{1+c_{\nu}}\alpha\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{1}}-\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{2}}\right)\right\}^{c_{\nu}}\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{r;n;k}}{2}\right),

(71)

c_{\nu}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\nu=y,\\
2,&\text{if }\nu=z,\end{cases}

(72)

\theta_{r;n;k}=2\Delta tw_{n;k}h_{x;r}\left(t_{k}\right),

(73)

“tfc” being an initialism for “transverse field components”,

h_{x;r}\left(t\right)
being the transverse field energy scale for
site
r
at time
t
,

I_{\nu;r;n}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)=\prod_{q_{2}=0}^{q_{\nu;n}}\prod_{q_{1}=0}^{q_{2}}\left\{I_{\nu;r;n;q_{1},q_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{2}}\right)\right\},

(74)

\displaystyle I_{\nu;r;n;q_{1},q_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{\left(l_{1},l_{2}\right)\in\Upsilon_{\nu;n;q_{1},q_{2}}}\left\{e^{-\gamma_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{2}}\right)}\right\},

(75)

\Upsilon_{y;n;q_{1},q_{2}}=\left\{\left(q_{1},q_{2}\right)\right\},

(76)

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{z;n;q_{1},q_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=\begin{cases}\left\{\left(0,0\right)\right\},&\text{if }q_{1}=q_{2}=0,\\
\left\{\left(0,2q_{2}-1\right),\left(0,2q_{2}\right)\right\},&\text{if }q_{1}=0\text{ and }1\leq q_{2}\leq n,\\
\left\{\left(0,2n+1\right)\right\},&\text{if }q_{1}=0\text{ and }q_{2}=n+1,\\
\left\{\left(2q_{1}-1,2q_{2}-1\right),\left(2q_{1},2q_{2}-1\right),\left(2q_{1}-1,2q_{2}\right),\left(2q_{1},2q_{2}\right)\right\},&\text{if }1\leq q_{1}<q_{2}\leq n,\\
\left\{\left(2q_{1}-1,2q_{2}-1\right),\left(2q_{1}-1,2q_{2}\right),\left(2q_{1},2q_{2}\right)\right\},&\text{if }1\leq q_{1}=q_{2}\leq n,\\
\left\{\left(2q_{1}-1,2n+1\right),\left(2q_{1},2n+1\right)\right\},&\text{if }1\leq q_{1}\leq n\text{ and }q_{2}=n+1,\\
\left\{\left(2n+1,2n+1\right)\right\},&\text{if }q_{1}=q_{2}=n+1,\end{cases}

(77)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathcal{E}_{\nu;r;l_{1}}^{\left(\lambda\right)}\mathcal{E}_{\nu;r;l_{2}}^{\left(\lambda\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{2}}-\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left\{\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}}-\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}}\right)\text{Re}\left[\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{2},l_{1}}\right]+i\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;1;q_{1}}+\sigma_{\nu;r;-1;q_{1}}\right)\text{Im}\left[\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{2},l_{1}}\right]\right\},

(78)

\mathcal{E}_{\nu;r;l}^{\left(\lambda\right)}=\mathcal{E}_{\nu;r}^{\left(\lambda\right)}\left(t=\left\lfloor l/2\right\rfloor\Delta t\right),

(79)

\mathcal{E}_{\nu;r}^{\left(\lambda\right)}\left(t\right)

being the energy scale associated with the coupling between the environment
and the
\nu
-component of the spin at site
r
[introduced in
Eq. (
10
)],

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\,A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)\frac{f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)}{\omega^{2}},

(80)

f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=\begin{cases}f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right),&\text{if }l_{1}-l_{2}=0,\\
f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right),&\text{if }l_{1}-l_{2}\geq 1,\end{cases}

(81)

f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)=2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}}{2}\right),

(82)

f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}}{2}\right),

(83)

\tilde{w}_{n;l}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{4},&\text{if }l=0,1,2n,2n+1,\\
\frac{1}{2},&\text{if }1<l<2n,\end{cases}

(84)

\sum_{l=a}^{b}F_{l}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }a>b,\\
F_{a}+F_{a+1}+\ldots+F_{b}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(85)

and
F_{l}
being an arbitrary sequence. For a detailed
discussion on how to evaluate numerically
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
,
see Appendix
D
.

III.6
Memory of the system

Because the system is coupled to its environment, the dynamics of
the system not only depend on its current state, but also its history.
This should be clear from the path integral representation of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

that we derived in Appendix
B
.
The degree to which the dynamics depend on the history of the system
can be characterized by a quantity known as the bath correlation time,
or “memory”. Informally, one may define the system’s memory
\tau

as the time beyond which both the
y
- and the
z
-components of
the bath correlation function [see Eqs. (
31
)-(
34
)]
are negligibly small:

\left|C_{\nu;T}\left(t>\tau\right)\right|\ll 1.

(86)

All information regarding the non-Markovian dynamics of
the system is encoded in the
\eta_{\nu;n;k_{1},k_{2}}
, which appear
in the path integral representation of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

[see Eqs. (
48
)-(
85
)
– in particular Eq. (
80
)].
A system with finite memory implies that there exists an integer
K_{\tau}>0

such that the
\eta_{\nu;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
for
2n+1\geq l_{1}\geq l_{2}+2K_{\tau}

contribute negligibly to the dynamics. In Appendix
D

we determine lower bounds for the
K_{\tau}
given the system’s memory

\tau
, namely that

K_{\tau}=\max\left(0,\left\lceil\frac{\tau-\frac{7}{4}\Delta t}{\Delta t}\right\rceil\right)+3,

(87)

and
\Delta t
being the time step size. We return to the
topic of memory in Sec.
IV
, where
we develop the tensor network algorithm used to calculate the path
integral representation of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)
.

At this point, we have discussed all the model components that define
the bath/environment. These components are the unit cell size
L
,
the inverse temperature
\beta
, the system’s memory
\tau
, the
energy scales
\mathcal{E}_{\nu;r}^{\left(\lambda\right)}\left(t\right)

associated with the couplings between the environment and the spins
[introduced in Eq. (
10
)],
and the zero-temperature limits
A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)

of the spectral densities of noise [introduced in Sec.
III.4
].
In
spinbosonchain
, this bath model component set is represented
by the
spinbosonchain.bath.Model
class.

IV
Tensor network algorithm

In this section, we describe in detail the tensor network algorithm
used to calculate the path integral representation of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

[Eqs. (
48
)-(
85
)].
The method we develop here draws from Refs.
(
Strathearn et al. 2018
;
Suzuki et al. 2019
)
.

IV.1
\tilde{\sigma}
variables

To reduce notational complexity, we map the Ising spin variable
\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r\in\left[0,L-1\right];\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}

to a variable
\tilde{\sigma}_{r\in\left[0,L-1\right];\alpha=\pm 1;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}
:

\tilde{\sigma}_{r;\alpha;m}\equiv\sigma_{\tilde{\nu}_{m};r;\alpha;\tilde{q}_{m}},

(88)

where

\Delta m=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
3,&\text{otherwise};\end{cases}

(89)

\tilde{\nu}_{m}=\begin{cases}z,&\text{if }m\text{ mod }3=0\text{ or }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
y,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(90)

and

\tilde{q}_{m}=\begin{cases}m,&\text{if }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
\left(2-\left\lfloor\frac{\left\{m+2\right\}\text{ mod }3}{2}\right\rfloor\right)\left\lfloor\frac{m}{3}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{\left(m\text{ mod }3\right)+1}{3}\right\rfloor,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(91)

We also introduce the vector notation:

\tilde{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}_{\alpha;m}=\left(\tilde{\sigma}_{r=-NL;\alpha;m},\ldots,\tilde{\sigma}_{r=NL+L-1;\alpha;m}\right),

(92)

where as a reminder,
2N+1
is the number of unit cells.

IV.2
Base-4 variables

To reduce notational complexity even further, we map the ordered pair

\left(\tilde{\sigma}_{r;1;m},\tilde{\sigma}_{r;-1;m}\right)
to
a variable
j_{r;m}
with four possible values:

\left(\tilde{\sigma}_{r;1;m},\tilde{\sigma}_{r;-1;m}\right)\to\begin{cases}\left(1,1\right),&\text{if }j_{r;m}=0,\\
\left(1,-1\right),&\text{if }j_{r;m}=1,\\
\left(-1,1\right),&\text{if }j_{r;m}=2,\\
\left(-1,-1\right),&\text{if }j_{r;m}=3.\end{cases}

(93)

By introducing the function
g_{\alpha}
:

g_{\alpha}\left(j\right)=\begin{cases}1-2\left\lfloor j/2\right\rfloor,&\text{if }\alpha=1,\\
1-2\left(j\mod 2\right),&\text{if }\alpha=-1.\end{cases}

(94)

we may write

\tilde{\sigma}_{r;\alpha;m}=g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r;m}\right).

(95)

We also introduce the vector notation:

\mathbf{j}_{m}=\left(j_{r=-NL;m},\ldots,j_{r=NL+L-1;m}\right).

(96)

and extend the definition of
g_{\alpha}
to accept
\mathbf{j}_{m}

vectors:

g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{j}_{m}\right)=\left(g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r=-NL;m}\right),\ldots,g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r=NL+L-1;m}\right)\right).

(97)

In
spinbosonchain
, the module
spinbosonchain._base4

contains functions for mapping between Ising variable pairs
\left(\tilde{\sigma}_{r;1;m},\tilde{\sigma}_{r;-1;m}\right)

and base-4 variables
j_{r;m}
.

IV.3
Rearranging terms in the path integral expression of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

In this section, we rearrange terms in the path integral expression
of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)
[Eq. (
48
)]
to make the subsequent discussion regarding the tensor network algorithm
easier to follow by the reader. By rearranging terms in Eq. (
48
),
and taking into account the system’s memory [see Sec.
III.6
],
we may rewrite the path integral expression of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

as:

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{1}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)z\right|\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\left|g_{-1}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left[\sum_{b_{r+uL;0}=0}^{3}\prod_{m=0}^{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL;m}=0}^{3}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\mathop{\times}\mathring{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)\mathring{I}_{n}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0};\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left\{k\Delta m\right\}_{k=0}^{q_{z;n}}}\right)}+\mathcal{O}\left[\Delta t^{2}\right],

(98)

where
\Delta m
is given by Eq. (
89
),
we have used the compact
j
-notation introduced in Secs.
IV.1

and
IV.2
;

\mathbf{b}_{0}=\left(b_{r=-NL;0},\ldots,b_{r=NL+L-1;0}\right);

(99)

\mathring{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)=\rho^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(g_{1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right),g_{-1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)\right),

(100)

with
g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{j}\right)
given by Eqs. (
94
)
and (
97
);

\mathring{\phi}_{n}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left\{k\Delta m\right\}_{k=0}^{q_{z;n}}}\right)=\sum_{k=0}^{q_{z;n}}\sum_{\alpha=\pm 1}\phi_{\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)\right),

(101)

with
\phi_{\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{j}_{3k}\right)\right)

given by Eq. (
55
);

\mathring{I}_{n}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0};\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)\right\},

(102)

with
\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)

being the influence functional that couples the base-
4
variables

j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}
with one another
[including base-
4
variables associated with different times]:

\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)=\mathring{I}_{r;n\Delta m-1}^{\left(1\right)}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,n\Delta m-1\right]}\right)\mathring{I}_{r;n}^{\left(2\right)}\left(j_{r;m\in\left[\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau},\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right),

(103)

\mathring{I}_{r;0}^{\left(1\right)}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;0}\right)=\delta_{b_{r;0},j_{r;0}}\mathring{I}_{r;n;0}\left(j_{r;0}\right),

(104)

\mathring{I}_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\left(1\right)}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,m_{2}+1\right]}\right)=\mathring{I}_{r;n=\infty;m_{2}+1}\left(j_{r;m\in\left[\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1\right]}\right)\mathring{I}_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,m_{2}\right]}\right),

(105)

\mathring{I}_{r;n}^{\left(2\right)}\left(j_{r;m\in\left[\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau},\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)=\prod_{m_{2}=n\Delta m}^{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\left\{\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m\in\left[\mu_{m_{2};\tau},m_{2}\right]}\right)\right\},

(106)

\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m\in\left[\mu_{m_{2};\tau},m_{2}\right]}\right)=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2};\tau}}^{m_{2}}\left\{\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)\right\},

(107)

\mu_{m;\tau}=\max\left(0,m-K_{\tau}\Delta m+1\right),

(108)

K_{\tau}
given by Eq. (
596
),

\tau
being the system’s “memory”,

\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=\begin{cases}\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{z-noise+tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{yz-noise+tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(109)

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{yz-noise+tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\begin{cases}\mathring{I}_{z;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}\text{ mod }3=0\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=0,\\
\mathring{I}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}=m_{2}-1\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=0,\\
1,&\text{if }m_{1}\neq m_{2}-1\text{ \& }m_{1}\text{ mod }3\neq 0\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=0,\\
1,&\text{if }m_{1}\text{ mod }3=0\text{ \& }m_{1}\neq m_{2}-1\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=1,\\
\mathring{I}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}=m_{2}-1\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=1,\\
\mathring{I}_{y;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}\text{ mod }3\neq 0\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=1,\\
1,&\text{if }m_{1}\text{ mod }3=0\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=2,\\
\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{tfc+y-bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}=m_{2}-1\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=2,\\
\mathring{I}_{y;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}\neq m_{2}-1\text{ \& }m_{1}\text{ mod }3\neq 0\text{ \& }m_{2}\text{ mod }3=2,\end{cases}

(110)

\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{z-noise+tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=\begin{cases}\mathring{I}_{z;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}\neq m_{2}-1,\\
\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{tfc+z-bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),&\text{if }m_{1}=m_{2}-1,\end{cases}

(111)

\mathring{I}_{\nu;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=I_{\nu;r;n;\tilde{q}_{m_{1}},\tilde{q}_{m_{2}}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right),g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right)\right),

(112)

I_{\nu;r;n;q_{1},q_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(\cdots\right)

given by Eq. (
75
),

\tilde{q}_{m}
given by Eq. (
91
),

\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{tfc+y-bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=\mathring{I}_{y;r;n;k=\frac{1}{2}\tilde{q}_{m_{1}}}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)\mathring{I}_{y;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),

(113)

\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{tfc+z-bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=\mathring{I}_{z;r;n;k=\tilde{q}_{m_{1}}}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)\mathring{I}_{z;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),

(114)

\mathring{I}_{\nu;r;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=I_{\nu;r;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right),g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right)\right),

(115)

I_{\nu;r;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\cdots\right)

given by Eq. (
70
),

\mathring{I}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right),g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right)\right),

(116)

\mathring{I}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)=I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{2}}\right),g_{1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right),g_{-1}\left(j_{r;m_{1}}\right)\right),

(117)

and
I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(\cdots\right)

and
I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(\cdots\right)
given
by Eqs. (
64
) and (
65
)
respectively.

In
spinbosonchain
, the quantities
\mathring{I}_{\nu;r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)
,

\mathring{I}_{\nu;r;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)
,

\mathring{I}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)
,

\mathring{I}^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)
,
and
\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)

are represented by instances of the classes

spinbosonchain._influence.twopt.Bath
,
spinbosonchain._influence.twopt.TF
,

spinbosonchain._influence.twopt.YZ
,
spinbosonchain._influence.twopt.ZY
,
and

spinbosonchain._influence.twopt.Total
respectively.

IV.4
Tensor network representation of the influence functionals

In this section, we show how the influence functional
\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)

given by Eq. (
103
) can be calculated
efficiently using tensor networks.

First, we introduce the two rank-
3
tensors:

\left[M_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}};0}^{j_{r;m_{2}}}=\delta_{b_{r;m_{2}},j_{r;m_{2}}}\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{2},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{2}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),

(118)

\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}}}=1,

(119)

where
\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right)

is given by Eq. (
110
).
We also introduce four rank-
4
tensors:

\left[W_{r;n;m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2};\tau},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;b_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}+1}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}}^{\prime}}=\delta_{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}},j_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}}^{\prime}}\mathring{I}_{r;n;\mu_{m_{2};\tau},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}},b_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}+1}\right),

(120)

\left[W_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}};j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}=\delta_{b_{r;m_{1}},b_{r;m_{1}+1}}\delta_{j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{1}},b_{r;m_{1}+1}\right),\quad\text{for }\mu_{m_{2};\tau}+1\leq m_{1}\leq m_{2}-1,

(121)

\left[W_{r;n;m_{1}=m_{2},m_{2}<\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};0}^{j_{r;m_{1}};j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}=\delta_{b_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{1}}}\delta_{j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{2},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{2}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),

(122)

and

\left[W_{r;n;m_{1}=m_{2},m_{2}=\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}};j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}=\delta_{b_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{1}}}\delta_{j_{r;m_{1}},j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\delta_{j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime},b_{r;m_{1}+1}}\mathring{I}_{r;n;m_{2},m_{2}}\left(j_{r;m_{2}},j_{r;m_{2}}\right),

(123)

where
\mu_{m;\tau}
is given by Eq. (
108
).
Note that any tensor indices labelled “
0
” indicate trivial
indices that are of dimension
1
. In
spinbosonchain
,
the tensors
M_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}
and
W_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}

are built by instances of the “factory” classes
spinbosonchain._influence.tensorfactory.InfluenceNodeRank3

and

spinbosonchain._influence.tensorfactory.InfluenceNodeRank4

respectively.

The rank-
4
tensors introduced above are used to construct MPO’s
of the form:

\left[\Omega_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}}=0;b_{r;m_{2}+1}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2};\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2};\tau}+1}^{m_{2}}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m_{1}}}\right\}\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2};\tau}}^{m_{2}}\left\{\left[W_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}};j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\right\}.

(124)

Note that from Eqs. (
107
)
and (
118
)-(
124
),
we have:

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{r;n=\infty;m_{2}+1}\left(j_{r;m\in\left[\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1\right]}\right)F\left(j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{m=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;m}^{\prime}}\right\}\left[\Omega_{r;n=\infty;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}=0;b_{r;m_{2}+2}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}F\left(j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1},j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}\right)\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}},

(125)

where
F\left(j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}\right)
is an
arbitrary function. In
spinbosonchain
, the MPO’s
\Omega_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}

are built by instances of the “factory” classes
spinbosonchain._influence.tensorfactory.InfluenceMPO
.

Next, we show how the influence functional
\mathring{I}_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,m_{2}\right]}\right)

[Eq. (
105
)] can
be calculated using MPS’s and MPO’s in an interative fashion. The

\mathring{I}_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,m_{2}\right]}\right)

can be expressed generally as two MPS’s that are joined together:

\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;m_{2}+1}=0}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}=\sum_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}\left[\Xi_{r;0,\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1}}\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime};b_{r;m_{2}+1}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}},

(126)

where
\Xi_{r;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}

is the first MPS:

\left[\Xi_{r;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{2}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}=\begin{cases}\prod_{m=m_{1}+1}^{m_{2}}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m}}\right\}\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}}\left\{\left[M_{r;m}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m};b_{r;m+1}}^{j_{r;m}}\right\},&\text{if }m_{1}\leq m_{2},\\
\delta_{b_{r;m_{1}},b_{r;m_{2}+1}},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(127)

with the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;m}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}

determined from previous steps in the iteration procedure; and
\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

is the second MPS:

\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;m_{2}+1}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{m_{2}}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m_{1}}}\right\}\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}}\left\{\left[M_{r;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}\right\},

(128)

with the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

determined from previous steps in the iteration procedure. It is important
to note that we use the following product notation convention:

\prod_{l=l_{1}}^{l_{2}}\left\{F_{l}\right\}=\begin{cases}F_{l_{1}}F_{l_{1}+1}\cdots F_{l_{2}},&\text{if }l_{1}\leq l_{2},\\
1,&\text{if }l_{1}>l_{2},\end{cases}

(129)

where
F_{l}
is an arbitrary sequence. The initial step
to our iterative procedure is to set:

\left[M_{r;0,0}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;1}}^{j_{r;0}}=\left[M_{r;n=\infty;0}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};0}^{j_{r;0}}.

(130)

The iterative step consists of calculating first:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;m_{2}+2}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;m}^{\prime}}\right\}\left[\Omega_{r;n=\infty;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m_{1}}}\right\}\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\left[M_{r;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}\right\},

(131)

where

\displaystyle\left[M_{r;m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}=b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}=\left(b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime}\right)}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n=\infty;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}\left[M_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}},

(132)

\displaystyle\left[M_{r;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}}=\left(b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}\right);b_{r;m_{1}+1}=\left(b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime}\right)}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}=\sum_{j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n=\infty;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime\prime};b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{1}};j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\left[M_{r;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime};b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}},\quad\text{for }\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1\leq m_{1}\leq m_{2}-1,

(133)

\left[M_{r;m_{1}=m_{2},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}}=\left(b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}\right);b_{r;m_{2}+1}=b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{2}}}=\sum_{j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n=\infty;m_{2},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime\prime};b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{2}};j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}\left[M_{r;m_{2},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime};0}^{j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}},

(134)

and

\left[M_{r;m_{1}=m_{2}+1,m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}+1}=b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime};b_{r;m_{2}+2}=0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}}=\sum_{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n=\infty;m_{2}+1,m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime};0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1};j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}.

(135)

The internal bond dimensions associated with the indices

\left\{b_{r;m}\right\}_{m=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{m_{2}}
in
\Xi_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}

[Eq. (
131
)]
are four times larger than those in
\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

[Eq. (
128
)],
therefore in order to avoid an exponential growth in the bond dimensions
upon repeated applications of the iterative procedure, one must compress

\Xi_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}
. For a brief
discussion on the compression techniques available in
spinbosonchain
,
see Appendix
E
. After
performing the MPS compression, we set

\left[M_{r;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}=\left[M_{r;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}},\quad\text{for }\mu_{m_{2}+2;\tau}\leq m_{1}\leq m_{2}+1,

(136)

and

\left[M_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}}=\left[M_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}},\quad\text{if }\mu_{m_{2}+2;\tau}\geq 1,

(137)

which completes the iterative step.

Note that Eqs. (
126
)-(
137
)
imply that

\left[\Xi_{r;0}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;1}=0}^{j_{r;0}}=\left[M_{r;n=\infty;0}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};0}^{j_{r;0}},

(138)

and

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;m_{2}+2}=0}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{b_{r;m_{2}+1}}\prod_{m=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;m}^{\prime}}\right\}\left[\Omega_{r;n=\infty;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};0}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1},j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}.

(139)

Furthermore, Eqs. (
108
),
(
118
), (
119
),
(
125
),
(
138
)
and (
139
)
imply that

\left[\Xi_{r;0}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;1}=0}^{j_{r;0}}=\delta_{b_{r;0},j_{r;0}}\mathring{I}_{r;n;0,0}\left(j_{r;0},j_{r;0}\right),

(140)

and

\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;m_{2}+2}=0}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}}=\mathring{I}_{r;n=\infty;m_{2}+1}\left(j_{r;m\in\left[\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1\right]}\right)\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};0}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}.

(141)

Upon comparing Eqs. (
140
)
and (
141
)
to Eqs. (
104
) and (
105
),
it should be straightforward to see that

\left[\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};0}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}=\mathring{I}_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,m_{2}\right]}\right).

(142)

Strictly speaking, the “
=
” symbol in Eq. (
142
)
should be “
\approx
” when compression is performed: the larger
the bond dimension, the more accurate the approximation.

The reason why
\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1\right)}
is expressed
as two MPS’s joined together in Eq. (
126
)
is that the left MPS
\Xi_{r;0,\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}

is not used in the iterative step. This fact will be exploited when
calculating the remainder of the path integral representation of
\rho^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};t_{n}\right)

[Eq. (
98
)], which we
discuss in Secs.
IV.5
-
IV.9
.

It is worth pointing out that
\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

[Eq. (
126
)], as an
MPS, can have no more than
N_{\max}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}=K_{\tau}\Delta m-1

nodes by design. At the beginning of the iterative procedure,
\Xi_{r;m_{2}=0}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

is an MPS of one node. Upon each iterative step,
\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

grows in size by one node, until it contains
N_{\max}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}

nodes. The number of nodes then remains fixed with subsequent iterative
steps, although the actual nodes making up
\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

will change.

Once
\Xi_{r;n\Delta m-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}
has been
calculated using the iterative procedure described above, we can use
it as input to another iterative procedure which is used to calculate

\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)

[Eq. (
103
)]. Below we describe
this second iterative procedure: First, we introduce the following
MPS, which itself is two MPS’s joined together:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};b_{r;m_{2}+1}=0}^{j_{r;n\Delta m},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}\left[\Xi_{r;n;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau},\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}^{j_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime};b_{r;m_{2}+1}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}},\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\text{for }n\Delta m-1\leq m_{2}\leq\left(n+1\right)\Delta m,

(143)

where
\Xi_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}

is the first MPS:

\left[\Xi_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{2}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}=\begin{cases}\prod_{m=m_{1}+1}^{m_{2}}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m}}\right\}\prod_{m=m_{1}}^{m_{2}}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m};b_{r;m+1}}^{j_{r;m}}\right\},&\text{if }m_{1}\leq m_{2},\\
\delta_{b_{r;m_{1}},b_{r;m_{2}+1}},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(144)

with the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;n;m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}

determined from previous steps in the iteration procedure; and
\Xi_{r;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}

is the second MPS:

\left[\Xi_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;m_{2}+1}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{m_{2}}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m_{1}}}\right\}\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}\right\},

(145)

with the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}

determined from previous steps in the iteration procedure. The initial
step to our iterative procedure is to set:

\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},n\Delta m-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}=\left[M_{r;m_{1},n\Delta m-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}},\quad\text{for }\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}\leq m_{1}\leq n\Delta m-1,

(146)

where the
M_{r;m_{1},n\Delta m-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

nodes are those of
\Xi_{r;n\Delta m-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,2\right)}

[see Eq. (
128
)].
The iterative step consists of calculating first:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{r;n;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;m_{2}+2}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;m}^{\prime}}\right\}\left[\Omega_{r;n;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;b_{r;m_{2}+2}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};0}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m_{1}}}\right\}\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{m_{2}+1}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}\right\},

(147)

where

\displaystyle\left[M_{r;n;m_{1}=\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}=b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}=\left(b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime}\right)}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}}\left[M_{r;n;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}^{\prime}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\prime}},

(148)

\displaystyle\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}}=\left(b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}\right);b_{r;m_{1}+1}=\left(b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime}\right)}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime\prime};b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{1}};j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}}\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime};b_{r;m_{1}+1}^{\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}^{\prime}},\quad\text{for }\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1\leq m_{1}\leq m_{2}-1,

(149)

\left[M_{r;n;m_{1}=m_{2},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}}=\left(b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime\prime},b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}\right);b_{r;m_{2}+1}=b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{2}}}=\sum_{j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n;m_{2},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime\prime};b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{2}};j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}}\left[M_{r;n;m_{2},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime};0}^{j_{r;m_{2}}^{\prime}},

(150)

and

\left[M_{r;n;m_{1}=m_{2}+1,m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}+1}=b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime};b_{r;m_{2}+2}=b_{r;m_{2}+2}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}}=\sum_{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}\left[W_{r;n;m_{2}+1,m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime\prime};b_{r;m_{2}+2}^{\prime\prime}}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1};j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}}\left[M^{\left(\mathring{I}\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{r;m_{2}+1}^{\prime}},

(151)

with

b_{r;m_{2}+2}^{\prime\prime}\in\begin{cases}\left\{0\right\},&\text{if }m_{2}+1<\left(n+1\right)\Delta m,\\
\left\{0,1,2,3\right\},&\text{if }m_{2}+1=\left(n+1\right)\Delta m.\end{cases}

(152)

Lastly, we compress the MPS
\Xi_{r;n;m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}

and set

\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}=\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}},\quad\text{for }\mu_{m_{2}+2;\tau}\leq m_{1}\leq m_{2}+1,

(153)

and

\left[M_{r;n;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}}=\left[M_{r;n;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau},m_{2}+1}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}};b_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}+1}}^{j_{r;\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}}},\quad\text{if }\mu_{m_{2}+2;\tau}\geq 1,

(154)

which completes the iterative step.

One can show, using similar arguments that lead to Eq. (
142
),
that

\displaystyle\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{b_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\prime}}\sum_{b_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m+1}}\left[\Xi_{r;0,\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}-1}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\prime}}^{j_{r;0},\ldots,j_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}-1}}\left[\Xi_{r;n;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\prime};b_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m+1}}^{j_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}},

(155)

where
\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)

is the entire influence functional for site
r
at time
t_{n}=n\Delta t

[given by Eqs. (
98
), (
103
)-(
117
)].
For later convenience, we also express
\Xi_{r;n;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv\right)}

[Eqs. (
143
)-(
145
)]
as

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{r;n;m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};b_{r;m_{2}+1}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;m_{2}}}
