\displaystyle=\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}+1}^{m_{2}}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;m_{1}}}\right\}\prod_{m_{1}=\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{m_{2}}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}\right\},

\displaystyle\quad\text{for }n\Delta m-1\leq m_{2}\leq\left(n+1\right)\Delta m,

(156)

\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}=\begin{cases}\left[M_{r;n;m_{1}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}},&\text{if }\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}\leq m_{1}\leq\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}-1,\\
\left[M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}\right]_{b_{r;m_{1}};b_{r;m_{1}+1}}^{j_{r;m_{1}}}&\text{if }\mu_{m_{2}+1;\tau}\leq m_{1}\leq m_{2},\end{cases}

(157)

where the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;n;m_{1}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,1\right)}

and
M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,2\right)}
are
determined from the iterative procedures described above.

In
spinbosonchain
, influence paths/functionals
\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(b_{r;0};j_{r;m\in\left[0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)

are represented by

spinbosonchain._influence.path
objects.

IV.5
Rearranging terms in the path integral expression of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

further

We now return to the path integral expression of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

that we introduced in Eq. (
98
)
and rearrange terms in that expression further to facilitate our subsequent
discussion of the remainder of the tensor network algorithm. Using
Eqs. (
98
), (
127
),
(
155
)-(
157
),
we can rearrange terms in the path integral expression of
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)

as follows:

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{1}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)z\right|\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\left|g_{-1}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left[\sum_{b_{r+uL;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}=0}^{3}\prod_{m=\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL;m}=0}^{3}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\mathop{\times}e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n}^{\left(\text{lcafc},\dashv\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left\{k\Delta m\right\}_{k=q_{n;\tau}}^{q_{z;n}}}\right)}\mathring{I}_{n}^{\left(\dashv\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left[\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau},\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\mathop{\times}\mathring{\rho}_{\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left[\Delta t^{2}\right],

(158)

where

q_{n;\tau}=\max\left(0,n-K_{\tau}+1\right),

(159)

with
K_{\tau}
given by Eq. (
87
),
and
\tau
being the system’s memory;
\mu_{m;\tau}
is given by
Eq. (
108
);
\Delta m
is given
by Eq. (
89
);

\mathring{\phi}_{n}^{\left(\text{lcafc},\dashv\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left\{k\Delta m\right\}_{k=q_{n;\tau}}^{q_{z;n}}}\right)=\sum_{k=q_{n;\tau}}^{q_{z;n}}\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right),

(160)

with

\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)=\sum_{\alpha=\pm 1}\phi_{\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)\right),

(161)

\phi_{\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)\right)

given by Eq. (
55
);

\mathring{I}_{n}^{\left(\dashv\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};\mathbf{j}_{m\in\left[\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau},\left(n+1\right)\Delta m\right]}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[\Xi_{r+uL;n;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};b_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m+1}=0}^{j_{r;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}},\ldots,j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\},

(162)

with the tensor
\Xi_{r;n;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv\right)}

being determined by the iteration procedures described in Sec.
IV.4
;

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;k\Delta m}=0}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\mathop{\times}\mathring{U}_{k}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1},\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)\mathring{\rho}_{k\Delta m+1}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1}\right),\quad\text{for }k\geq 0,

(163)

with

\mathring{\rho}_{0}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)=\mathring{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right),

(164)

\mathring{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)

given by Eq. (
100
);

\mathring{\rho}_{1}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;0}=0}^{3}\right\}\mathring{U}^{\left(\vdash,i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)\mathring{\rho}_{0}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right),

(165)

\mathring{U}^{\left(\vdash,i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL;0}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;0}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r+uL;0};b_{r+uL;1}}^{j_{r+uL;0}}\right\}e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n=\infty;0}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{0}\right)},

(166)

\displaystyle\mathring{U}_{k}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1},\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=2}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;k\Delta m+p}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+p};b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+p+1}}^{j_{r+uL;k\Delta m+p}}\right\}e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n=\infty;k+1}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}\right)}.

(167)

and the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;m}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}

being determined from the first iteration procedure described in Sec.
IV.4
.

We can continue to rearrange terms as follows:

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{1}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)z\right|\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\left|g_{-1}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;n\Delta m+1}=0}^{3}\right\}\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(f\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{n\Delta m+1},\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)\mathring{\rho}_{n;n\Delta m+1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{n\Delta m+1}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left[\Delta t^{2}\right],

(168)

where

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}_{n;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;k\Delta m}=0}^{3}\right\}\mathring{U}_{n;k}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1},\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)\mathring{\rho}_{n;k\Delta m+1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\text{for }\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}\leq k\Delta m+1\leq n\Delta m-2,

(169)

with

\mathring{\rho}_{n;1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;0}=0}^{3}\right\}\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)\mathring{\rho}_{0}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right),\quad\text{for }1\leq n\leq K_{\tau}-1,

(170)

\mathring{\rho}_{n;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}\left(\mathbf{b}_{\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}\right)=\mathring{\rho}_{\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}\right),\quad\text{for }n\geq K_{\tau},

(171)

\mathring{\rho}_{m}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{m}\right)

being determined by Eqs. (
163
)-(
167
),

\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL;0}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;n;0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r+uL;0};b_{r+uL;1}}^{j_{r+uL;0}}\right\}e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n;0}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{0}\right)},

(172)

\displaystyle\mathring{U}_{n;k}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1},\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=2}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;k\Delta m+p,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r;k\Delta m+p};b_{r;k\Delta m+p+1}}^{j_{r;k\Delta m+p}}\right\}e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n;k+1}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}\right)},

(173)

and

\displaystyle\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(f\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{n\Delta m+1},\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;n\Delta m+p}^{\prime}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=2}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;n\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m-1}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;n\Delta m+p,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r;n\Delta m+p};b_{r;n\Delta m+p+1}}^{j_{r;3n+p}^{\prime}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[M_{r;n;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};b_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m+1}=j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime}}e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n;n+1}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime}\right)}.

(174)

with the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}

being determined by the iteration procedures described in Sec.
IV.4
.

In the following four sections, we describe the remainder of the tensor
network algorithm while making several references to this current
section.

IV.6
MPS representation of the initial state operator

Let us now express the system’s reduced density matrix at
t=0
[see
Eqs. (
51
),
(
53
),
(
94
), (
96
),
(
97
), and (
100
)],

\mathring{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)=\left\langle g_{1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)z\vphantom{\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}}\right.\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle\left\langle\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right|\left.g_{-1}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)z\vphantom{\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}}\right\rangle,

(175)

as a MPS. As a reminder,
g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)

is defined by Eqs. (
94
) and (
97
),
and
\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle
is a pure state
vector. As a pure state vector,
\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle

can be expressed as a MPS
(
Schollwöck 2011
)
:

\displaystyle\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle g_{\alpha}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)z\vphantom{\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}}\right.\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{a_{\alpha;uL;0}}\sum_{a_{\alpha;\left(u+1\right)L;0}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Xi_{u}^{\left(\rho,i,A\right)}\right]_{a_{\alpha;uL;0};a_{\alpha;\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{g_{\alpha}\left(b_{uL;0}\right),\ldots,g_{\alpha}\left(b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}\right)}\right\},

(176)

where

\left[\Xi_{u}^{\left(\rho,i,A\right)}\right]_{a_{\alpha;uL;0};a_{\alpha;\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{g_{\alpha}\left(b_{uL;0}\right),\ldots,g_{\alpha}\left(b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}\right)}=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{a_{\alpha;r+uL;0}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL}^{\left(\Psi,i,A\right)}\right]_{a_{\alpha;r+uL;0};a_{\alpha;r+uL+1;0}}^{g_{\alpha}\left(b_{r+uL;0}\right)}\right\},

(177)

and the
a_{\alpha;r;0}
are bond indices. Note that
a_{\alpha;-NL;0}

and
a_{\alpha;\left(N+1\right)L;0}
are trivial bond indices:

a_{\alpha;-NL;0},a_{\alpha;\left(N+1\right)L;0}\in\left\{0\right\},

(178)

Typically,
\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle

is an instantaneous ground of the system Hamiltonian
\hat{H}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t=0\right)

[Eqs. (
3
)-(
5
)],
in which case we can use the density matrix renormalization group
(DMRG) method
(
Schollwöck 2011
)
to calculate
\left|\Psi^{\left(i,A\right)}\right\rangle

in the form of a MPS. Using Eqs. (
175
)
and (
176
), we can write:

\mathring{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;0}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Xi_{u}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},i,A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}\right\},

(179)

where

\left[\Xi_{u}^{\left(\rho,i,A\right)}\right]_{a_{\alpha;uL;0};a_{\alpha;\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{g_{\alpha}\left(b_{uL;0}\right),\ldots,g_{\alpha}\left(b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}\right)}=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;0}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},i,A\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;0};c_{r+uL+1;0}}^{b_{r+uL;0}}\right\},

(180)

and

\displaystyle\left[M_{r+uL}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},i,A\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;0}=\left(a_{1;r+uL;0},a_{-1;r+uL;0}\right);c_{r+uL+1;0}=\left(a_{1;r+uL+1;0},a_{-1;r+uL+1;0}\right)}^{b_{r+uL;0}}

\displaystyle\quad=\left[M_{r+uL}^{\left(\Psi,i,A\right)}\right]_{a_{1;r+uL;0};a_{1;r+uL+1;0}}^{g_{1}\left(b_{r+uL;0}\right)}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL}^{\left(\Psi,i,A\right)}\right]_{a_{-1;r+uL;0};a_{-1;r+uL+1;0}}^{g_{-1}\left(b_{r+uL;0}\right)}\right\}^{*}.

(181)

IV.7
MPS representation of
e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)}

In this section, we show how to express
e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)}

[see Eqs. (
55
)-(
58
),
(
161
)]
as a MPS.

Using Eqs. (
55
)-(
58
),
(
161
),
we express
\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)

as:

\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)=\sum_{u=-N}^{N}\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\mathring{\phi}_{r+uL;n;k}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}\left(j_{r+uL;k\Delta m}\right)+\mathring{\phi}_{r+uL,r+uL+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}\left(j_{r+uL;k\Delta m},j_{r+uL+1;k\Delta m}\right)\right\},

(182)

where

\mathring{\phi}_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right)=-\frac{\Delta t}{2}\sum_{\alpha=\pm 1}\alpha\sum_{k^{\prime}=k-1}^{k}w_{n;k^{\prime}}H_{z;r}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}\left(t_{k^{\prime}};g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right)\right);

(183)

\mathring{\phi}_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m},j_{r+1;k\Delta m}\right)=-\frac{\Delta t}{2}\sum_{\alpha=\pm 1}\alpha\sum_{k^{\prime}=k-1}^{k}w_{n;k^{\prime}}H_{z;r,r+1}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}\left(t_{k^{\prime}};g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right),g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r+1;k\Delta m}\right)\right);

(184)

H_{z;r}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}\left(t;g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right)\right)=h_{z;r}\left(t\right)g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right);

(185)

H_{z;r,r+1}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}\left(t;g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right),g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r+1;k\Delta m}\right)\right)=J_{z,z;r,r+1}\left(t\right)g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right)g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r+1;k\Delta m}\right);

(186)

\Delta m
and
g_{\alpha}\left(\cdots\right)
are defined
by Eqs. (
89
) and (
94
).
Next, we introduce two rank-2 tensors:

\left[X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>};j_{r+1;k\Delta m}^{<}}=e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>},j_{r+1;k\Delta m}^{<}\right)},

(187)

\left[X_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m};j_{r;k\Delta m}^{\prime}}=\delta_{j_{r;k\Delta m},j_{r;k\Delta m}^{\prime}}e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}\left(j_{r;k\Delta m}\right)},

(188)

and split
X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}
into
two rank-2 tensors using e.g. QR decomposition:

\displaystyle\left[X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>};j_{r+1;k\Delta m}^{<}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{c_{r+1;k\Delta m}}\left[X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},>\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>};c_{r+1;k\Delta m}}\left[X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},<\right)}\right]_{c_{r+1;k\Delta m};j_{r+1;k\Delta m}^{<}},

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\text{for }-NL\leq r\leq\left(N+1\right)L-2.

(189)

where if one used QR decomposition then
X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},>\right)}

and
X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},<\right)}
are orthogonal and
upper triangular matrices respectively. For convenience, we also define

X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},<\right)}
and
X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},>\right)}

for
r=-NL-1
and
r=\left(N+1\right)L-1
respectively:

\left[X_{-NL-1,-NL;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},<\right)}\right]_{c_{-NL;k\Delta m};j_{-NL;k\Delta m}^{<}}=1,

(190)

\left[X_{\left(N+1\right)L-1,\left(N+1\right)L;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},>\right)}\right]_{j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m}^{>};c_{\left(N+1\right)L;k\Delta m}}=1,

(191)

where

j_{r;k\Delta m}^{<}\in\begin{cases}\left\{0\right\},&\text{if }r=-NL,\\
\left\{0,1,2,3\right\},&\text{if }r>-NL,\end{cases}

(192)

j_{r;k\Delta m}\in\left\{0,1,2,3\right\},\quad\forall r.

(193)

j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>}\in\begin{cases}\left\{0,1,2,3\right\},&\text{if }r<\left(N+1\right)L-1,\\
\left\{0\right\},&\text{if }r=\left(N+1\right)L-1,\end{cases}

(194)

and

c_{r=-NL;k\Delta m},c_{r=\left(N+1\right)L;k\Delta m}\in\left\{0\right\}.

(195)

We also introduce a rank-3 tensor:

\left[M_{r}^{\left(\delta\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{<};j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>}}^{j_{r;k\Delta m}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }L+N=1,\\
\delta_{j_{r;k\Delta m},j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>}},&\text{if }r=-NL\text{ and }L+N>1,\\
\delta_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{<},j_{r;k\Delta m}},&\text{if }r=\left(N+1\right)L-1\text{ and }L+N>1,\\
\delta_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{<},j_{r;k\Delta m}}\delta_{j_{r;k\Delta m},j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>}},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(196)

In
spinbosonchain
, the tensors
X_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}

and
X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc}\right)}
are built by instances
of the “factory” classes

spinbosonchain._phasefactor.tensorfactory.ZFieldPhaseFactorNodeRank2

and

spinbosonchain._phasefactor.tensorfactory.ZZCouplerPhaseFactorNodeRank2
.

Next, we construct a new rank-3 tensor for the tensors introduced
in Eqs. (
189
)-(
196
):

\displaystyle\left[M_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}\right]_{c_{r;k\Delta m};c_{r+1;k\Delta m}}^{j_{r;k\Delta m}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{<}=0}^{3}\sum_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{\prime}=0}^{3}\sum_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>}=0}^{3}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[X_{r-1,r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},<\right)}\right]_{c_{r;k\Delta m};j_{r;k\Delta m}^{<}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[X_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lfc}\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m};j_{r;k\Delta m}^{\prime}}\left[M_{r}^{\left(\delta\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{<};j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>}}^{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[X_{r,r+1;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcc},>\right)}\right]_{j_{r;k\Delta m}^{>};c_{r+1;k\Delta m}}.

(197)

Using the
M_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}
tensors
we can express
e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)}

as a MPS:

e^{i\mathring{\phi}_{n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{k\Delta m}\right)}=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;k\Delta m}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;k\Delta m}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Xi_{u;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;k\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;k\Delta m}}^{j_{uL;k\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m}}\right\},

(198)

where

\left[\Xi_{u;n;k}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;k\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;k\Delta m}}^{j_{uL;k\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m}}=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;k\Delta m}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;k\Delta m};c_{r+uL+1;k\Delta m}}^{j_{r+uL;k\Delta m}}\right\}.

(199)

IV.8
MPO representations of
\mathring{U}^{\left(\vdash,i\right)}
,
\mathring{U}_{k}^{\left(\vdash\right)}
,

\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(i\right)}
,
\mathring{U}_{n;k}
, and
\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(f\right)}

In this section, we show how to express
\mathring{U}^{\left(\vdash,i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)

[Eq. (
166
)],
\mathring{U}_{k}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{3k+1},\mathbf{b}_{3k+4}\right)

[Eq. (
167
)],
\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)

[Eq. (
172
)],
\mathring{U}_{n;k}\left(\mathbf{b}_{3k+1},\mathbf{b}_{3k+4}\right)

[Eq. (
173
)], and
\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(f\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{3n+1},\mathbf{j}_{3n+3}\right)

[Eq. (
174
)] as MPO’s.

Using Eqs. (
166
), (
198
),
and (
199
), we can express

\mathring{U}^{\left(\vdash,i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)

by the following MPO:

\mathring{U}^{\left(\vdash,i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;0}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Omega_{u}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash,i\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1};b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}\right\},

(200)

where

\left[\Omega_{u}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash,i\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1};b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;0}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[W_{r+uL}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash,i\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;0};c_{r+uL+1;0}}^{b_{r+uL;1};b_{r+uL;0}}\right\},

(201)

with

\left[W_{r}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash,i\right)}\right]_{c_{r;0};c_{r+1;0}}^{b_{r;1};b_{r;0}}=\sum_{j_{r;0}=0}^{3}\left[M_{r;n=\infty;0}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}\right]_{c_{r;0};c_{r+1;0}}^{j_{r;0}}\left[M_{r;0}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;1}}^{j_{r;0}},

(202)

the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;m}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}

being determined from the first iteration procedure described in Sec.
IV.4
,
and the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;n;k}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}

being determined from the procedure described in Sec.
IV.7
.

Using Eqs. (
167
) and (
199
),
we can express
\mathring{U}_{k}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{3k+1},\mathbf{b}_{3k+4}\right)

by the following MPO:

\displaystyle\mathring{U}_{k}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1},\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Omega_{u;k}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}}\right\},

(203)

where

\displaystyle\left[\Omega_{u;k}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[W_{r+uL;k}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+uL+1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+1}}\right\},

(204)

with

\displaystyle\left[W_{r;k}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{r;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{r;k\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{p=2}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\left[M_{r;k\Delta m+p}^{\left(\mathring{I},1,1\right)}\right]_{b_{r;k\Delta m+p};b_{r;k\Delta m+p+1}}^{j_{r;k\Delta m+p}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[M_{r;n=\infty;k+1}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{r;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}},

(205)

and
\Delta m
being defined by Eq. (
89
).

Using Eqs. (
172
) and (
199
),
we can express
\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)

by the following MPO:

\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(i\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0},\mathbf{b}_{1}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;0}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Omega_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},i\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1};b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}\right\},

(206)

where

\left[\Omega_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},i\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1};b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;0}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[W_{r+uL;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},i\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;0};c_{r+uL+1;0}}^{b_{r+uL;1};b_{r+uL;0}}\right\},

(207)

with

\left[W_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},i\right)}\right]_{c_{r;0};c_{r+1;0}}^{b_{r;1};b_{r;0}}=\sum_{j_{r;0}=0}^{3}\left[M_{r;n;0}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}\right]_{c_{r;0};c_{r+1;0}}^{j_{r;0}}\left[M_{r;n;0,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv\right)}\right]_{b_{r;0};b_{r;1}}^{j_{r;0}},

(208)

and the rank-
3
tensors
M_{r;n;m_{1},m_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}

being determined by the iteration procedures described in Sec.
IV.4
.

Using Eqs. (
173
) and (
199
),
we can express
\mathring{U}_{n;k}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1},\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)

by the following MPO:

\displaystyle\mathring{U}_{n;k}\left(\mathbf{b}_{k\Delta m+1},\mathbf{b}_{\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Omega_{u;n;k}^{\left(\mathring{U}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}}\right\},

(209)

where

\displaystyle\left[\Omega_{u;n;k}^{\left(\mathring{U}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[W_{r+uL;n;k}^{\left(\mathring{U}\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+uL+1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+1}}\right\},

(210)

with

\displaystyle\left[W_{r;n;k}^{\left(\mathring{U}\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+uL+1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{p=2}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;k\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;k\Delta m+p,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r;k\Delta m+p};b_{r;k\Delta m+p+1}}^{j_{r;k\Delta m+p}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[M_{r;n;k+1}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{r;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}.

(211)

Using Eqs. (
174
) and (
199
),
we can express
\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(f\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{3n+1},\mathbf{j}_{3n+3}\right)

by the following MPO:

\displaystyle\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(f\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{n\Delta m+1},\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Omega_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},f\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};b_{uL;n\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;n\Delta m+1}}\right\},

(212)

where

\displaystyle\left[\Omega_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},f\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};b_{uL;n\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;n\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[W_{r+uL;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},f\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+uL+1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{r+uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};b_{r+uL;n\Delta m+1}}\right\},

(213)

with

\displaystyle\left[W_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},f\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};b_{r;n\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{j_{r;n\Delta m+p}^{\prime}=0}^{3}\right\}\prod_{p=2}^{\Delta m}\left\{\sum_{b_{r;n\Delta m+p}=0}^{3}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{p=1}^{\Delta m}\left\{\left[M_{r;n;n\Delta m+p,\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\left(\mathring{I},\dashv,\right)}\right]_{b_{r;n\Delta m+p};b_{r;n\Delta m+p+1}}^{j_{r;n\Delta m+p}^{\prime}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[M_{r;n;n+1}^{\left(\text{lacfc}\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{r+1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime}}.

(214)

Note that
b_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}
is set to
j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}

in calculating
W_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},f\right)}
and
\mathring{U}_{n}^{\left(f\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{n\Delta m+1},\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)
.

IV.9
Remaining elements of tensor network algorithm

All that remains is to show how we implement Eqs. (
163
)-(
165
)
and (
168
)-(
171
)
using tensor networks. In MPS notation, we can express
\mathring{\rho}_{m}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{m}\right)
,

\mathring{\rho}_{n;m}\left(\mathbf{b}_{m}\right)
, and
\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

as:

\mathring{\rho}_{m}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{m}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;m}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;m}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Xi_{u;m}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;m}}^{b_{uL;m},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;m}}\right\},

(215)

\mathring{\rho}_{n;m}\left(\mathbf{b}_{m}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;m}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;m}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Xi_{u;n;m}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;m}}^{b_{uL;m},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;m}}\right\},

(216)

\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}.

(217)

Equation (
164
) is equivalent to

\left[\Xi_{u;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}=\left[\Xi_{u}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},i,A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}},

(218)

where
\Xi^{\left(\mathring{\rho},i,A\right)}
is the MPS
representing
\mathring{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{0}\right)
,
which is determined by the procedure described in Sec.
IV.6
.

Equation (
165
) is equivalent to
the following MPO application to a MPS:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{u;1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;1}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;0}=0}^{3}\right\}\left[\Omega_{u}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash,i\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0}^{\prime};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}^{\prime}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1};b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{u;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}},

*Perform MPS compression

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;1}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;1};c_{r+uL+1;1}}^{b_{r+uL;1}}\right\},

(219)

where the
M_{r;1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}

are the rank-
3
tensors resulting from the application of the MPO

\Omega_{u}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash,i\right)}
[see Eqs. (
200
)-(
205
)]
to the MPS
\Xi_{u;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}
followed
by MPS compression [see Appendix
E

for references on compression techniques used in
spinbosonchain
].

Equation (
163
) is
equivalent to the following MPO application to a MPS:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{u;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+1}=0}^{3}\right\}\left[\Omega_{u;k}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{u;k\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;k\Delta m+1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;k\Delta m+1}}^{b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}},

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\text{*Perform MPS compression}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}^{b_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}\right\},\quad\text{for }k\geq 0,

(220)

where the
M_{r;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}

are the rank-
3
tensors resulting from the application of the MPO

\Omega_{u;k}^{\left(\mathring{U},\vdash\right)}
[see Eqs. (
203
)-(
205
)]
to the MPS
\Xi_{u;k\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}

followed MPS compression.

Equation (
163
) is
equivalent to the following MPO application to a MPS:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{u;n;1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;1}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;0}=0}^{3}\right\}\left[\Omega_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},i\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0}^{\prime};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}^{\prime}}^{b_{uL;1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;1};b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{u;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;0};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;0}}^{b_{uL;0},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;0}},

*Perform MPS compression

\displaystyle=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;1}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;n;1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{r+uL;1};c_{r+uL+1;1}}^{b_{r+uL;1}}\right\},\quad\text{for }1\leq n\leq K_{\tau}-1,

(221)

where
K_{\tau}
is given by Eq. (
87
),

\tau
is the system’s memory, and the
M_{r;n;1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}

are the rank-
3
tensors resulting from the application of the MPO

\Omega_{n}^{\left(\mathring{U},i\right)}
[see Eqs. (
206
)-(
208
)]
to the MPS
\Xi_{u;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}
followed
by MPS compression.

Equation (
171
) is equivalent
to

\left[\Xi_{u;n;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}}^{b_{uL;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}}=\left[\Xi_{u;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}}^{b_{uL;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}}},

(222)

where
\mu_{m;\tau}
is given by Eq. (
108
).

Equation (
163
) is
equivalent to the following MPO application to a MPS:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{u;n;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;k\Delta m+1}=0}^{3}\right\}\left[\Omega_{u;n;k}^{\left(\mathring{U}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m}}^{b_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{u;n;k\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;k\Delta m+1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;k\Delta m+1}}^{b_{uL;k\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;k\Delta m+1}},

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\text{*Perform MPS compression}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;n;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}^{b_{r+uL;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}}\right\},

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\text{for }\mu_{n\Delta m;\tau}\leq k\Delta m+1\leq n\Delta m-2,

(223)

where the
M_{r;n;\left(k+1\right)\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}

are the rank-
3
tensors resulting from the application of the MPO

\Omega_{u;n;k}^{\left(\mathring{U}\right)}
[see Eqs. (
209
)-(
211
)]
to the MPS
\Xi_{u;n;k\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}

followed MPS compression.

Lastly, Eq. (
168
) is equivalent
to the following MPO application to a MPS:

\displaystyle\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{b_{r+uL;n\Delta m+1}=0}^{3}\right\}\left[\Omega_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},f\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime}}^{j_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m+1},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m+1};b_{uL;n\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;n\Delta m+1}}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{u;n;n\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;n\Delta m+1};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;n\Delta m+1}}^{b_{uL;n\Delta m+1},\ldots,b_{\left(u+1\right)L-1;n\Delta m+1}},

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\text{*Perform MPS compression}

\displaystyle\quad=\prod_{r=1}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{r+uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\left[M_{r+uL;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};c_{\left(u+1\right)L;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{r+uL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\},

(224)

where the
M_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
are
the rank-
3
tensors resulting from the application of the MPO
\Omega_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{U},f\right)}

[see Eqs. (
212
)-(
214
)]
to the MPS
\Xi_{u;n;n\Delta m+1}^{\left(\mathring{\rho}\right)}

followed by MPS compression.

In
spinbosonchain
, a given system’s reduced density matrix

\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

is represented by an instance of the
spinbosonchain.state.SystemState

class. This class handles the evolution of
\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

via the
spinbosonchain.state.SystemState.evolve
method.

IV.10
Computable quantities

There are some useful quantities that can be calculated straightforwardly
using the MPS algorithm described in the previous sections. The first
quantity we consider is the trace of the system’s reduced state operator

\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)
. Stricly speaking,
due to the way MPS’s are normalized upon truncations or applications
of MPO’s in
spinbosonchain
,
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)

might not have a unit trace. Therefore it is important that we normalize

\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)
by its trace upon
calculating any expectation values of observables. Using Eqs. (
93
),
(
94
), (
96
),
(
97
), (
98
),
and (
217
), the trace can
be expressed as

\text{Tr}^{\left(A\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}=\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\left[X_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\},

(225)

where

\left[X_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}=\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0,3}\right\}\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{j_{uL},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1}},

(226)

with
\Delta m
is given by Eq. (
89
).
We can rewrite Eq. (
225
) in the following
form:

\text{Tr}^{\left(A\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}=\sum_{c_{0}}\sum_{c_{L}}\left[v_{n;N;N}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{0}}\left[X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{0};c_{L}}\left[v_{n;N;N}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{L}}.

(227)

where

\left[v_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{0}}=\prod_{u=-N}^{-N+\tilde{N}-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\left[X_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\},

(228)

\left[v_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{L}}=\prod_{u=N-\tilde{N}+1}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\left[X_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\},

(229)

and as a reminder we are using the product convention given
by Eq. (
129
). It is useful to rewrite
Eqs. (
228
)
and (
229
)
as:

\left[v_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{0}}=C_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}x_{n;N;0}^{\tilde{N}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{0}},

(230)

\left[v_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{L}}=C_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}x_{n;N;0}^{\tilde{N}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{L}},

(231)

where

\lim_{\tilde{N}\to\infty}C_{n;N\geq\tilde{N};\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}=C_{n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)},

(232)

\lim_{\tilde{N}\to\infty}C_{n;N\geq\tilde{N};\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}=C_{n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)},

(233)

with
\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}
,

\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}
,
C_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}
,

C_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}
, and

x_{n;N;0}
to be determined. Recall from Sec.
III.1

that in
spinbosonchain
, we set
N=0
for finite systems
and take
N\to\infty
for infinite systems. For finite systems, it
is clear from Eq. (
129
) that

\left[\tilde{v}_{n;0;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{0}}=\left[\tilde{v}_{n;0;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{L}}=C_{n;0;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}=C_{n;0;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}=1,

(234)

where

c_{0},c_{L}\in\left\{0\right\},\quad\text{for }N=0.

(235)

For infinite systems, Eqs. (
228
)
and (
229
)
are just instances of unnormalized left and right power iterations
respectively, as should be apparent from the fact that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\left[X_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{0};c_{L}}=\left[X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{0};c_{L}},\quad\text{for finite }u.

(236)

If we assume that there is only one dominant eigenvalue
of
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
[i.e. that there
is only one eigenvalue that is largest in magnitude], then as
N\to\infty
,

x_{n;N;0}
converges to the dominant eigenvalue of
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}

whereas
\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}

and
\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}
converge
to left and right dominant eigenvectors respectively:

\sum_{c_{0}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{0}}\left[X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{0};c_{L}}=x_{n;N;0}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{L}},\quad\text{as }N\to\infty,

(237)

\sum_{c_{L}}\left[X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{0};c_{L}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{L}}=x_{n;N;0}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{0}},\quad\text{as }N\to\infty.

(238)

The assumption that the dominant eigenvalue is non-degenerate
is typically valid, however strictly speaking there is no guarantee
that this assumption is valid
(
Schollwöck 2011
;
Orús and Vidal 2008
)
. Nevertheless,
we assume this in
spinbosonchain
, as is often done in
practice
(
Schollwöck 2011
)
. We can normalize
\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}

and
\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}
such
that

\sum_{c}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c}=1.

(239)

As we show below, we do not need to determine either
C_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}

or
C_{n;N;\tilde{N}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}
in
order to calculate any expectation values. From the above discussion,
it follows that

\text{Tr}^{\left(A\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}=C_{n;N;N}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}C_{n;N;N}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}x_{n;N;0}^{2N+1},\quad\text{for }N=0\text{ or }N\to\infty.

(240)

The next quantity we consider is the expectation value of an operator

\hat{O}\left(t\right)
at time
t=t_{n}
, where
\hat{O}\left(t\right)

is an operator in the Schrödinger picture [implying possible intrinsic
time dependence]. Using Eqs. (
93
),
(
94
), (
96
),
(
97
), (
98
),
and (
217
), we can write

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}

\displaystyle\equiv\frac{1}{\text{Tr}^{\left(A\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}}\text{Tr}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\text{Tr}^{\left(A\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0}^{3}\right\}\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{j_{uL},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}O\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{-NL}},\ldots,\overline{j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1}}\right),

(241)

where

\overline{j}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }j_{r}=0,\\
2,&\text{if }j_{r}=1,\\
1,&\text{if }j_{r}=2,\\
3,&\text{if }j_{r}=3,\end{cases}

(242)

and

\displaystyle O\left(t_{n};j_{-NL},\ldots,j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\left\langle g_{1}\left(j_{-NL}\right)z;\ldots;g_{1}\left(j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1}\right)z\right|\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\left|g_{-1}\left(j_{-NL}\right)z;\ldots;g_{-1}\left(j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1}\right)z\right\rangle,

(243)

with
g_{\alpha}\left(j\right)
defined by Eq. (
94
),

\left|g_{\alpha}\left(j_{-NL}\right)z;\ldots;g_{\alpha}\left(j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1}\right)z\right\rangle=\bigotimes_{u=-N}^{N}\bigotimes_{r=0}^{L-1}\left|g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r+uL}\right)z\right\rangle,

(244)

and

\hat{\sigma}_{z;r}\left|g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r}\right)z\right\rangle=g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r}\right)\left|g_{\alpha}\left(j_{r}\right)z\right\rangle.

(245)

If
\hat{O}
is a finite string of single-spin operators:

\hat{O}\left(t\right)=\bigotimes_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\bigotimes_{r=0}^{L-1}\hat{O}_{r+uL}\left(t\right),

(246)

where

-N\leq u_{i}\leq 0\leq u_{f}\leq N,

(247)

then Eq. (
241
)
becomes:

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{C_{n;N;N}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}C_{n;N;N}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}x_{n;N;0}^{2N+1}}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left[v_{n;N;N+u_{i}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0}^{3}O_{r+uL}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{r+uL}}\right)\right\}\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{j_{uL},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1}}\right\}\left[v_{n;N;N-u_{f}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}},

(248)

where

O_{r}\left(t_{n};j_{r}\right)=\left\langle g_{1}\left(j_{r}\right)z\right|\hat{O}_{r}\left|g_{-1}\left(j_{r}\right)z\right\rangle,

(249)

and we used Eq. (
240
). From
Eqs. (
230
),
(
231
),
(
234
),
(
235
), and (
247
),
it should be clear that for finite systems Eq. (
248
)
reduces to:

\left\langle\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}=\frac{1}{x_{n;0;0}}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r}=0}^{3}O_{r}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{r}}\right)\right\}\left[\Xi_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{0;0}^{j_{0},\ldots,j_{L-1}},\quad\text{for }N=0.

(250)

For infinite system, we can use Eqs. (
247
)-(
247
),
(
236
)-(
239
),
and (
239
) to show that Eq. (
248
)
reduces as follows:

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\cancel{C_{n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}}\cancel{C_{n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}}x_{n;N;0}^{2N+1}}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left\{\cancel{C_{n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}}x_{n;N;0}^{N+u_{i}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0}^{3}O_{r+uL}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{r+uL}}\right)\right\}\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{j_{uL},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left\{\cancel{C_{n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}}x_{n;N;0}^{N-u_{f}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x_{n;N;0}^{u_{f}-u_{i}+1}}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0}^{3}O_{r+uL}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{r+uL}}\right)\right\}\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{j_{uL},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1}}\right\}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}},

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\text{for }N\to\infty.

(251)

The result of Eq. (
250
)
is a special case of Eq. (
251
),
hence we may write:

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x_{n;N;0}^{u_{f}-u_{i}+1}}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0}^{3}O_{r+uL}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{r+uL}}\right)\right\}\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{j_{uL},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1}}\right\}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}},

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\text{for }N=0\text{ or }N\to\infty.

(252)

In
spinbosonchain
, the function
spinbosonchain.ev.multi_site_spin_op

calculates
\left\langle\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}

for a given
\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)
of the form given by Eq. (
246
).
The implementation of the function
spinbosonchain.ev.multi_site_spin_op

is based on Eq. (
252
).
This function can be used to calculate the expectation value of strings
of Pauli spin operators
\hat{\sigma}_{\nu;r}
and the identity operator.

Another application of Eq. (
252
)
is to calculate classical spin probabilities. Given a classical spin
configuration of a subset of the system’s spins:

\left(\sigma_{z;u_{i}L},\ldots\sigma_{z;\left(u_{f}+1\right)L-1}\right),

(253)

with

\sigma_{z;r}=\pm 1,

(254)

we can calculate the probability of measuring the
z
-components
this subset of spin to be in the given configuration
\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;u_{i},u_{f}}

at time
t_{n}
by setting

\hat{O}\left(t_{n}\right)=\bigotimes_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\bigotimes_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\frac{1+\sigma_{z;r+uL}\hat{\sigma}_{z;r+uL}}{2}\right\},

(255)

and using Eq. (
252
)
to get

\displaystyle\text{Prob}\left(t_{n};\sigma_{z;u_{i}L},\ldots\sigma_{z;\left(u_{f}+1\right)L-1}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{x_{n;N;0}^{u_{f}-u_{i}+1}}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L}}

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0}^{3}O_{r+uL}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{r+uL}}\right)\right\}\left[\Xi_{u;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{\frac{3}{2}\left(1-\sigma_{z;uL}\right),\ldots,\frac{3}{2}\left(1-\sigma_{z;\left(u+1\right)L-1}\right)}\right\}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}},

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\quad\text{for }N=0\text{ or }N\to\infty.

(256)

In
spinbosonchain
, the function
spinbosonchain.state.spin_config_prob

calculates

\text{Prob}\left(t_{n};\sigma_{z;u_{i}L},\ldots\sigma_{z;\left(u_{f}+1\right)L-1}\right)
.

We can also use Eq. (
252
)
to calculate the expectation value of the system’s energy per unit
cell. To see how this can be done, first note that

\left\langle\hat{\varepsilon}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}\equiv\frac{1}{2N+1}\left\langle\hat{H}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}=\left\langle\hat{H}_{u=0}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}},\quad\text{for }N=0\text{ or }N\to\infty,

(257)

where we have used Eqs. (
1
)
and (
2
). Using Eqs. (
3
)-(
5
),
we can rewrite Eq. (
257
) as

\displaystyle\left\langle\hat{\varepsilon}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{h_{x;r}\left(t_{n}\right)\left\langle\hat{\sigma}_{x;r}\right\rangle_{t_{n}}+h_{z;r}\left(t_{n}\right)\left\langle\hat{\sigma}_{z;r}\right\rangle_{t_{n}}+J_{z,z;r,r+1}\left(t_{n}\right)\left\langle\hat{\sigma}_{z;r}\hat{\sigma}_{z;r+1}\right\rangle_{t_{n}}\right\},

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\text{for }N=0\text{ or }N\to\infty.

(258)

From Eq. (
258
), it should be
clear that we can apply Eq. (
252
)
to calculate
\left\langle\hat{\varepsilon}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}
.
In
spinbosonchain
, the function
spinbosonchain.ev.energy

calculates
\left\langle\hat{\varepsilon}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\rangle_{t_{n}}
,
doing so by calling
spinbosonchain.ev.single_site_spin_op

and
spinbosonchain.ev.nn_two_site_spin_op
: the former
calculates expectation values of single-site spin operators
\hat{\sigma}_{\nu;r}
,
and the latter calculates the expectation values of nearest-neighbour
two-site spin operators
\hat{\sigma}_{\nu_{1};r}\hat{\sigma}_{\nu_{2};r+1}
.

Next, we use Eq. (
252
)
to derive an expression for characteristic correlation lengths of
two-point correlators for infinite systems. Let us consider the following
coarse-grained two-point correlation function:

G_{u_{i},u_{f}}\left(t_{n}\right)=\left\langle\hat{O}_{u_{i}}^{\left(1\right)}\hat{O}_{u_{f}}^{\left(2\right)}\right\rangle_{t_{n}},

(259)

where
\hat{O}_{u_{i}L}^{\left(1\right)}
and
\hat{O}_{u_{f}L}^{\left(2\right)}

are multi-site operators that operate on the unit cells
u=u_{i}

and
u=u_{f}
respectively. From Eq. (
252
),
we can see that
G_{u_{i},u_{f}}\left(t_{n}\right)
can be expressed
as

\displaystyle G_{u_{i},u_{f}}\left(t_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x_{n;N;0}^{u_{f}-u_{i}+1}}\prod_{u=u_{i}}^{u_{f}}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+u_{i}L}=0}^{3}\right\}O_{u_{i}}^{\left(1\right)}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{u_{i}L}},\ldots,\overline{j_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L-1}}\right)\left[\Xi_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L};c_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L}}^{j_{u_{i}L},\ldots,j_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L-1}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{u=u_{i}+1}^{u_{f}-1}\left\{\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+uL}=0,3}\right\}\left[\Xi_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}^{j_{uL},\ldots,j_{\left(u+1\right)L-1}}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+u_{f}L}=0}^{3}\right\}O_{u_{f}}^{\left(2\right)}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{u_{f}L}},\ldots,\overline{j_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L-1}}\right)\left[\Xi_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{f}L};c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}}^{j_{u_{f}L},\ldots,j_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L-1}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}}

\displaystyle=x_{n;N;0}^{-u_{f}+u_{i}+1}\prod_{u=u_{i}+1}^{u_{f}-1}\left\{\sum_{c_{uL}}\sum_{c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left[v_{n;N}^{\left(O,1\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L}}\prod_{u=u_{i}+1}^{u_{f}-1}\left\{\left[X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{uL};c_{\left(u+1\right)L}}\right\}\left[v_{n;N}^{\left(O,2\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}\right)L}},

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\text{for }N\to\infty,

(260)

where

\displaystyle\left[v_{n;N}^{\left(O,1\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x_{n;N;0}}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+u_{i}L}=0}^{3}\right\}O_{u_{i}}^{\left(1\right)}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{u_{i}L}},\ldots,\overline{j_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\sum_{c_{u_{i}L}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L}}\left[\Xi_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{i}L};c_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L}}^{j_{u_{i}L},\ldots,j_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L-1}},

(261)

\displaystyle\left[v_{n;N}^{\left(O,2\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}\right)L}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{x_{n;N;0}}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r+u_{f}L}=0}^{3}\right\}O_{u_{f}}^{\left(2\right)}\left(t_{n};\overline{j_{u_{f}L}},\ldots,\overline{j_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\sum_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}}\left[\Xi_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{u_{f}L};c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}}^{j_{u_{f}L},\ldots,j_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L-1}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}+1\right)L}}.

(262)

If we assume that
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}

is not a defective matrix, then we can expand
v_{n;N}^{\left(O,1\right)}

and
v_{n;N}^{\left(O,2\right)}
in terms of left and right eigenbases
of
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
respectively such that
for each left eigenvector from the left eigenbasis, there is exactly
one right eigenvector from the right eigenbasis to which it is not
orthogonal. Moreover, this right eigenvector shares the same eigenvalue
as the given left eigenvector and their dot product is unity. Mathematically,
if we denote
\left\{\tilde{v}_{n;N;l}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right\}_{l=0}^{\chi_{s}-1}

and
\left\{\tilde{v}_{n;N;l}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right\}_{l=0}^{\chi_{s}-1}

as the aforementioned left and right eigenbases respectively, then
we have:

\left[v_{n;N}^{\left(O,1\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L}}=\sum_{l}\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,1\right)}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;l}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{i}+1\right)L}},

(263)

\left[v_{n;N}^{\left(O,2\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}\right)L}}=\sum_{l}\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,2\right)}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;l}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{\left(u_{f}\right)L}},

(264)

where
\left(\chi_{s}-1\right)\times\left(\chi_{s}-1\right)

are the dimensions of the matrix
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
;

\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,1\right)}
and
\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,2\right)}

are expansion coefficients which we need not determine for the purposes
of this discussion; and

\sum_{c_{0}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;l}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{0}}\left[X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{0};c_{L}}=x_{n;N;l}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;l}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c_{L}},

(265)

\sum_{c_{L}}\left[X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}\right]_{c_{0};c_{L}}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;l}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{L}}=x_{n;N;l}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;0}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{0}},

(266)

\sum_{c}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;l_{1}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{c}\left[\tilde{v}_{n;N;l_{2}}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c}=\delta_{l_{1},l_{2}},

(267)

with
\left\{x_{n;N;l}\right\}_{l=0}^{\chi_{s}-1}
being
the eigenspectrum of
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
[note
that we have already denoted
x_{n;N;l=0}
as the dominant eigenvalue
of
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
]. The assumption
that
X_{0;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
is not a defective
matrix is typically valid, however stricly speaking there is no guarantee
that this assumption is valid. Nevertheless, we assume this in
spinbosonchain

for practicality. Using Eqs. (
263
)-(
267
),
we can rewrite Eq. (
260
)
as

\displaystyle G_{u_{i},u_{f}}\left(t_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\chi_{s}-1}\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,1\right)}\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,2\right)}\left\{\frac{x_{n;N;l}}{x_{n;N;0}}\right\}^{u_{f}-u_{i}-1}

\displaystyle=\psi_{n;N;0}^{\left(O,1\right)}\psi_{n;N;0}^{\left(O,2\right)}+\sum_{l=1}^{\chi_{s}-1}e^{i\varphi_{l}\left\{u_{f}-u_{i}-1\right\}}\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,1\right)}\psi_{n;N;l}^{\left(O,2\right)}e^{-\frac{L}{\xi_{l}}\left\{u_{f}-u_{i}-1\right\}},\quad\text{for }N\to\infty,

(268)

where

\varphi_{l}=\arg\left(\frac{x_{n;N;l}}{x_{n;N;0}}\right),

(269)

and

\xi_{l}=-\frac{L}{\ln\left(\left|x_{n;N;l}/x_{n;N;0}\right|\right)},\quad\text{for }1\leq l\leq\chi_{s}-1\text{ and }N\to\infty.

(270)

From Eq. (
268
),
we see that two-point correlators can be long-ranged if
\psi_{n;N;0}^{\left(O,1\right)}\psi_{n;N;0}^{\left(O,2\right)}\neq 0

and/or be made up of a superposition of exponentials with decay lengths

\xi_{l}
. These
\xi_{l}
are the correlation lengths mentioned
above. For infinite systems, the
\xi_{l}
are calculated automatically
upon evolving the system in
spinbosonchain
, and are stored
as the attribute
correlation_lengths
of the
spinbosonchain.state.SystemState

class.

Lastly, we consider entanglement measures. Even though the system
is prepared in a pure state, i.e.
\hat{\rho}^{\left(i,A\right)}

is a pure state operator [see Eq. (
14
)],
generally speaking
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)

will be a mixed-state operator since the system is coupled to an environment.
As a result, the bi-partite von Neumann entanglement entropy is not
a good entanglement measure for it cannot distinguish between classical
and quantum correlations, and a mixed-state can possess both, unlike
a pure state which can only possess quantum correlations. Below we
describe an alternative approach that works for mixed states. First,
we express
\Xi_{n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A\right)}
, the MPS representing

\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)
, as a mixed-canonical
MPS:

\displaystyle\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\left[\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{0;c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{-NL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{r-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\tilde{\Lambda}_{r;c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\left[\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};0}^{j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}},\quad\text{for }0\leq r\leq L-1,

(271)

where
\Lambda_{r;c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}
is the
Schmidt spectrum associated with the bond between sites
r-1
and

r
, and
\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}
and

\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}
satisfy

\displaystyle\prod_{r^{\prime}=-NL}^{r-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r^{\prime};\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}=0}^{3}\right\}\left\{\left[\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{0;c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}^{j_{-NL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{r-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\vdash\right)}\right]_{0;c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime}}^{j_{-NL;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{r-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}=\delta_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime}},

(272)

\displaystyle\prod_{r^{\prime}=r}^{\left(N+1\right)L-1}\left\{\sum_{j_{r^{\prime};\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}=0}^{3}\right\}\left\{\left[\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};0}^{j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}\right\}^{*}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[\Xi_{r;n}^{\left(\mathring{\rho},A,\dashv\right)}\right]_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m};0}^{j_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},\ldots,j_{\left(N+1\right)L-1;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}}=\delta_{c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m},c_{r;\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}^{\prime}}.

(273)

For detailed discussions on the mixed-canonical form of
a finite MPS see Ref.
(
Schollwöck 2011
)
, and for infinite systems
see Refs.
(
Orús and Vidal 2008
;
McCulloch 2008
;
Schollwöck 2011
)
. Let us normalize
the Schmidt spectra by the trace of the system’s reduced density matrix:

\Lambda_{r;c}=\frac{\tilde{\Lambda}_{r;c}}{\text{Tr}^{\left(A\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}}.

(274)

According to the realignment criterion
(
Chen and Wu 2003
;
Rudolph 2003
)
,
if
\sum_{c}\Lambda_{r;c}>1
then the system is in a bipartite entangled
state for the bipartition formed by splitting the chain in two between
sites
r-1
and
r
. It is important to note that
\sum_{c}\Lambda_{r;c}\leq 1

is a necessary condition for a state to be separable [i.e. not entangled]
for the aforementioned bipartition, however it is not
sufficient
.
Therefore, for a given bipartition of the chain, there exist bipartite
entangled states that violate
\sum_{c}\Lambda_{r;c}>1
. That being
said
\sum_{c}\Lambda_{r;c}\leq 1
is considered to be a strong condition
for separability
(
Chen and Wu 2003
;
Rudolph 2003
)
, hence the criterion
\sum_{c}\Lambda_{r;c}>1

should detect most bipartite entangled states. Unfortunately, we cannot
apply the realignment criterion to infinite chains since we cannot
determine
\text{Tr}^{\left(A\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\right\}
.

In
spinbosonchain
for finite systems, one can determine
if a given system state possesses any bipartite entanglement using
the
spinbosonchain.state.realignment_criterion
function.
The function checks for bipartite entanglement for every bipartition.

V
Conclusion

In this manuscript, we have extended the work of Refs.
(
Strathearn et al. 2018
;
Suzuki et al. 2019
;
Oshiyama et al. 2020
)

by developing a generalized QUAPI formalism that is capable of describing
the dynamics of a generalized spin-boson chain model, where both the

z
- and
y
-components of the quantum Ising spins are coupled to
bosonic baths, rather than only the
z
-components. We have derived
a path integral expression for the system’s reduced density matrix
and have developed a tensor network technique that enables us to evaluate
the path integral in a computationally efficient manner. Our formalism
is flexible in that it can handle both Markovian and non-Markovian
types of noise, which is particularly useful when studying hybrid
types of noise consisting of e.g. a
1/f
component and an ohmic
component. We have implemented our approach in a Python library called

spinbosonchain

(
D-Wave Systems 2021
)
.

One possible application of
spinbosonchain
is to study
the noisy dynamics of flux qubit chains subject to both charge and
flux noise. In this context, charge and flux noise are modelled by

y
- and
z
-noise respectively in our generalized spin-boson model.
In future work, we plan on using
spinbosonchain
to study
the Kibble-Zurek mechanism in flux-qubit chains in the presence of
charge and flux noise, and to connect with experimental results obtained
by D-Wave’s flux-qubit quantum annealers. Another possible future
direction of this work is to generalized our formalism to different
topologies, e.g. ladders, square lattices, chains with next-nearest
couplings, and so on. The formalism would only need to be modified
in Secs.
IV.7
and
IV.8
,
where the various
\mathring{U}
-tensors would have to be expressed
as MPO’s with bond dimensions greater than or equal to
4
, in contrast
to chain topologies where all MPO bond dimensions are equal to
4
.
Therefore, the simulation of more complicated topologies will be more
computationally expensive as compared to that of chain topologies.

Acknowledgements.

This work was supported by Mitacs through the Mitacs Accelerate program.

Appendix A
Coherent states

Let
\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}
be the bosonic
annihilation operator for a harmonic oscillator at site
r
in mode

\epsilon
with angular frequency
\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}
, coupled
to the
\nu^{\text{th}}
component of the spin at the same site.
We define the bosonic coherent states
\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle

as the set of normalized kets that are common eigenstates of the annihilation
operators
\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}
. These
bosonic coherent states can be expressed as
(
Negele and Orland 1998
)

\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle\equiv\sum_{\epsilon}\sum_{u=-N}^{N}\sum_{r=0}^{L-1}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{b_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon;\varphi}^{n\vphantom{\dagger}}}{n!}\left(\hat{b}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\dagger}\right)^{n}\left|\mathbf{0}_{\nu}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle,

(275)

where
2N+1
is the number of unit cells in the system
[see Sec.
III.1
for a brief discussion on unit cells],

L
is the number of sites in every unit cell,
\varphi
is a set
of labels [e.g.
\varphi=\{\},\varphi=\{j;\alpha;k\}
] used to
distinguish between different instances of the resolution of identity
[see below],
\left|\mathbf{0}_{\nu}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle

is the vacuum state, i.e. a state with no bosons, and the complex
numbers
b_{\nu;r;\epsilon;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}
make up
the components of the complex vector
\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}
,
which parameterizes the coherent state
\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle
.
Using Eq. (
275
), we
can verify that the coherent states
\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle

are right-handed eigenvectors of the annihilation operator
\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}
:

\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle=b_{\nu;r;\epsilon;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle.

(276)

The dual equation to Eq. (
276
)
is

\left\langle\mathbf{b}_{\nu}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right|\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\dagger}=\left\langle\mathbf{b}_{\nu}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right|b_{\nu;r;\epsilon;\varphi}^{*\vphantom{\dagger}}.

(277)

Equations (
276
)
and (
277
) further
imply that

f\left(\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}\right)\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle=f\left(b_{\nu;r;\epsilon;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\dagger}}\right)\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle,

(278)

\left\langle\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right|\hat{b}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\dagger}=\left\langle\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right|f\left(b_{\nu;r;\epsilon;\varphi}^{*\vphantom{\dagger}}\right),

(279)

where
f\left(\ldots\right)
denotes an arbitrary function.

Coherent states
\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle

can be used as basis vectors, but they are not orthonormal, as can
be seen by taking the inner product between two arbitrary coherent
states
\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle

and
\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\prime}\right\rangle

\left\langle\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi^{\vphantom{\varphi}}}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right|\left.\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi^{\prime}}^{\prime}\right\rangle=e^{\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi^{\vphantom{\varphi}}}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi^{\prime}}^{\prime}}\equiv\prod_{\epsilon}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{e^{b_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon;\varphi^{\vphantom{\prime}}}^{*\vphantom{\prime}}b_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon;\varphi^{\prime}}^{\vphantom{*}\prime}}\right\},

(280)

The coherent state vectors obey the following completeness
relation

\int d^{2}\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,e^{-\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}}\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle\left\langle\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right|=\prod_{\epsilon}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\hat{1}_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon}^{\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}\right\},

(281)

where

\displaystyle d^{2}\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}

\displaystyle\equiv\prod_{\epsilon}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{d^{2}b_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\}

\displaystyle\equiv\prod_{\epsilon}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left\{\frac{d\left(\text{Re}\left[b_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon;\varphi}^{\vphantom{*}}\right]\right)d\left(\text{Im}\left[b_{\nu;r+uL;\epsilon;\varphi}^{\vphantom{*}}\right]\right)}{\pi}\right\},

(282)

and
\hat{1}_{\nu;r;\epsilon}^{\left(\mathcal{B}\right)}

is the identity operator of the Hilbert space associated with the
harmonic oscillator at site
r
in mode
\epsilon
with angular
frequency
\omega_{\nu;\epsilon}
, coupled to the
\nu^{\text{th}}

component of the spin at the same site. The integration is over the
entire complex plane
\mathbb{C}
. The trace of an operator
\hat{A}

in the coherent state basis is given by

\text{Tr}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\hat{O}\right\}=\int d^{2}\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\,e^{-\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\cdot\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}}\left\langle\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right|\hat{O}\left|\mathbf{b}_{\nu;\varphi}^{\vphantom{\prime}}\right\rangle.

(283)

Appendix B
Deriving the path integral form of the system’s reduced density matrix

In this appendix, we present a derivation of one particular coherent
state path integral representation of the device’s reduced state operator

\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)
, drawing from references
Refs.
(
Strathearn et al. 2018
;
Suzuki et al. 2019
)
, but in certain respects we are
extending the work therein. Recall from Sec.
III

that
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)
can be expressed
as

\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t\right)=\text{Tr}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\hat{\rho}\left(t\right)\right\}=\text{Tr}^{\left(B\right)}\left\{\hat{U}\left(t,0\right)\hat{\rho}^{\left(i\right)}\hat{U}\left(0,t\right)\right\},

(284)

where
\hat{H}\left(t\right)
is given by Eqs. (
1
)-(
11
),
\hat{\rho}^{\left(i\right)}

is given by Eqs. (
13
)-(
16
),
(
17
),(
20
),
and (
23
), and
\hat{U}\left(t,0\right)

is given by Eq. (
26
).

Shortly down below, the time integrals in Eq. (
26
)
will be evaluated using the trapezoidal quadrature scheme on a discretized
uniform time grid
t_{k}
defined by

t_{k}=k\Delta t,

(285)

where
\Delta t
