I_{r;n}^{\left(\text{z-noise}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,\tilde{q}_{z;n}\right]}\right)=I_{z;r;n}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,\tilde{q}_{z;n}\right]}\right)I_{z;r;n}^{\left(\text{bath}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,\tilde{q}_{z;n}\right]}\right),

(560)

with

I_{z;r;n}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,\tilde{q}_{y;n}\right]}\right)=\prod_{l=0}^{n}\left\{I_{z;r;n;k=l}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;1;l},\sigma_{z;r;-1;l},\sigma_{z;r;1;l+1},\sigma_{z;r;-1;l+1}\right)\right\},

(561)

and

\displaystyle I_{z;r;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;1;l},\sigma_{z;r;-1;l},\sigma_{z;r;1;l+1},\sigma_{z;r;-1;l+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{\alpha=\pm 1}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\prod_{q=0}^{\tilde{q}_{y;n}}\left\{\sum_{\sigma_{y;r+uL;\alpha;q}=\pm 1}\right\}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1,2l},\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l},\sigma_{z;r;1,l},\sigma_{z;r;-1,l}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}I_{y;r;n;k=l}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1;2l},\sigma_{y;r;-1;2l},\sigma_{y;r;1;2l+1},\sigma_{y;r;-1;2l+1}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;1,2l+1},\sigma_{y;r;-1,2l+1},\sigma_{z;r;1,l+1},\sigma_{z;r;-1,l+1}\right).

(562)

From Eqs. (
540
)-(
546
)
we see that the
I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,2\right)}
and
I^{\left(y\leftrightarrow z,1\right)}

in Eq. (
562
)
transform
I_{y;r;n;k=l}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}
from the
\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q}
-basis
to the
\sigma_{z;r;\alpha;q}
-basis. Therefore we can rewrite Eq. (
562
)
as

\displaystyle I_{z;r;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;1;q_{1}},\sigma_{z;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{z;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{z;r;-1;q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad=I_{z;r;1;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;1;q_{2}},\sigma_{z;r;1;q_{1}}\right)I_{z;r;-1;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;-1;q_{1}},\sigma_{z;r;-1;q_{2}}\right),

(563)

where

I_{z;r;\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha;q_{1}},\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q_{2}}\right)=\begin{cases}\left\langle\sigma_{z;r;-1;q_{1}}z\right|e^{i\Delta tw_{n;k}h_{x;r}\left(t_{k}\right)\hat{\sigma}_{x;r}}\left|\sigma_{z;r;-1;q_{2}}z\right\rangle,&\text{if }\alpha=-1,\\
\left\langle\sigma_{z;r;1;q_{2}}z\right|e^{-i\Delta tw_{n;k}h_{x;r}\left(t_{k}\right)\hat{\sigma}_{x;r}}\left|\sigma_{z;r;1;q_{1}}z\right\rangle,&\text{if }\alpha=1.\end{cases}

(564)

Using Eqs. (
562
)
and (
564
),
and the exponential identity
(
Gottfried and Yan 2003
)
:

e^{-\frac{i}{1}\theta\hat{\sigma}_{x;r}}=\cos\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right)\hat{1}_{r}-i\hat{\sigma}_{x;r}\sin\left(\frac{\theta}{2}\right),

(565)

we can write

\displaystyle I_{\nu;r;\alpha;n;k}^{\left(\text{tfc}\right)}\left(\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q_{1}},\sigma_{y;r;\alpha;q_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{1}}+\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{2}}\right)^{2}\cos\left(\frac{\theta_{r;n;k}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\mathop{+}i^{1+c_{\nu}}\alpha\left\{\frac{1}{2}\left(\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{1}}-\sigma_{\nu;r;\alpha;q_{2}}\right)\right\}^{c_{\nu}}\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{r;n;k}}{2}\right),

(566)

c_{\nu}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if }\nu=y,\\
2,&\text{if }\nu=z,\end{cases}

(567)

\theta_{r;n;k}=2\Delta tw_{n;k}h_{x;r}\left(t_{k}\right).

(568)

In summary, the path integral expression for
\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)

in Eq. (
559
) can be
written concisely as:

\displaystyle\rho^{\left(A\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;n+1},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;n+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\left\langle\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;n+1}z\right|\hat{\rho}^{\left(A\right)}\left(t_{n}\right)\left|\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;n+1}z\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\prod_{\alpha=\pm 1}\prod_{u=-N}^{N}\prod_{r=0}^{L-1}\left[\prod_{q=0}^{q_{y;n}}\left\{\sum_{\sigma_{y;r+uL;\alpha;q}=\pm 1}\right\}\prod_{q=0}^{q_{z;n}-1}\left\{\sum_{\sigma_{z;r+uL;\alpha;q}=\pm 1}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}e^{i\sum_{\alpha=\pm 1}\phi_{\alpha;n}^{\left(\text{lcafc}\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;\alpha;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right)}I_{n}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\rho^{\left(i,A\right)}\left(\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;1;0},\boldsymbol{\sigma}_{z;-1;0}\right)+\mathcal{O}\left[\Delta t^{2}\right],

(569)

where

q_{y;n}=\begin{cases}-1,&\text{if }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
2n+1,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(570)

q_{z;n}=n+1,

(571)

and

I_{r;n}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right)=\begin{cases}I_{r;n}^{\left(\text{z-noise}\right)}\left(\sigma_{z;r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{z;n}\right]}\right),&\text{if }A_{y;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=0,\\
I_{r;n}^{\left(\text{yz-noise}\right)}\left(\sigma_{\nu\in\left\{y,z\right\};r;\alpha=\pm 1;q\in\left[0,q_{\nu;n}\right]}\right),&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(572)

which is the result presented in Sec.
III.5
.

Appendix C
Evaluating numerically the spectral densities of noise

As discussed in Sec.
III.4
, the
spectral density of the noise coupled to the
\nu
-component of the
spin at site
r
and temperature
T
,
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
,
will sometimes naturally breakdown into multiple components:

A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=\sum_{\varsigma}A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right),

(573)

where

A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)=\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}},

(574)

where
\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)
is the sign function
[defined in Eq. (
38
)],

\beta=1/\left(k_{B}T\right)
, and where
A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)

is the
\varsigma^{\text{th}}
component of
A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)
.
In this appendix, we discuss how to evaluate numerically
A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)
.
For convenience, we introduce the following shorthand notation:

f\left(a^{\pm}\right)\equiv\lim_{\omega\to a^{\pm}}f\left(\omega\right),

(575)

where
f\left(\omega\right)
is an arbitrary function,
and
a
is a real number. Since
A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)

has a removable singularity at
\omega=0
, we calculate
A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(0^{\pm}\right)

using L’Hôpital’s rule:

A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(0^{\pm}\right)=\pm\frac{\left.\frac{d}{d\omega}\left\{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\pm\omega\right)\right\}\right|_{\omega\to 0^{+}}}{\left.\frac{d}{d\omega}\left\{1-e^{-\beta\omega}\right\}\right|_{\omega\to 0^{\pm}}}=\frac{\left.\frac{d}{d\omega}\left\{A_{\nu;r;T=0}\left(\omega\right)\right\}\right|_{\omega\to 0^{+}}}{\beta}.

(576)

For finite
\omega
in the neighbourhood of
\omega=0
,
we perform a Taylor expansion of the denominator of Eq. (
574
)
to fifth order in
\beta\omega
:

\displaystyle A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\neq 0\right)

\displaystyle=\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}}

\displaystyle\approx\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{-\left\{-\beta\omega+\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}-\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{5}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)}{\beta\omega\left\{1-\frac{1}{2}\beta\omega+\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}+\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right\}}

\displaystyle\approx\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}\left\{1+\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}-\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}-\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}-\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]^{3}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}-\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]^{4}\right\}

\displaystyle\approx\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}\left\{1+\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}-\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\left[\frac{1}{4}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}+\frac{1}{36}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\left[\frac{1}{8}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}-\frac{1}{12}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}-\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]+\left[\frac{1}{16}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}\left\{1+\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)\right]+\left[\frac{1}{4}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\left[\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}+\frac{1}{8}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}-\frac{1}{6}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{3}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\left[-\frac{1}{120}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}+\frac{1}{36}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}+\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{\approx}\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}}\left.\phantom{\quad\mathop{+}}\left.\quad\mathop{-}\frac{1}{12}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}-\frac{1}{24}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}+\frac{1}{16}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}\left\{1+\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)+\frac{1}{12}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{720}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right\}.

(577)

In practice, we approximate
A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)

by Eq. (
577
)
for
0<\left|\omega\right|<10^{-3}/\beta
to avoid overflow problems.
For
\omega\leq-10^{-3}/\beta
, we rewrite
A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)

as

A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\leq-10^{-3}/\beta\right)=-\frac{e^{\beta\omega}A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(-\omega\right)}{e^{\beta\omega}-1},

(578)

to also avoid overflow problems. For
\omega\geq 10^{-3}/\beta
,
we evaluate
A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)
using Eq. (
574
):

A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\geq 10^{-3}/\beta\right)=\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}}.

(579)

It is also useful to specify hard infrared and ultraviolet
cutoff frequencies,
\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)}

and
\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)}
, for the
A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)

such that

A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|<\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)}\right)=0,

(580)

A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|>\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)}\right)=0,

(581)

where

0\leq\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)}\leq\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)}.

(582)

We can summarize the results of Eqs. (
576
)-(
582
)
by writing

A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)=\begin{cases}\frac{\left.\frac{d}{d\omega}\left\{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)\right\}\right|_{\omega\to 0^{+}}}{\beta},&\text{if }\omega=0^{\pm},\\
\text{sign}\left(\omega\right)\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|\right)}{\left(\beta\omega\right)}\left\{1+\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta\omega\right)+\frac{1}{12}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{720}\left(\beta\omega\right)^{4}\right\},&\text{if }0<\left|\omega\right|<10^{-3}/\beta,\\
-\frac{e^{\beta\omega}A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(-\omega\right)}{e^{\beta\omega}-1},&\text{if }\omega\leq-10^{-3}/\beta,\\
\frac{A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)}{1-e^{-\beta\omega}},&\text{if }\omega\geq 10^{-3}/\beta,\end{cases}

(583)

where

A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|<\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)}\right)=0,

(584)

A_{\nu;r;T=0;\varsigma}\left(\left|\omega\right|>\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)}\right)=0,

(585)

with

0\leq\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)}\leq\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)}.

(586)

Appendix D
How to evaluate numerically
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}

Considerable care must be taken when evaluating
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
,
which can be expressed as [see Eq. (
80
)]:

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\,A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)\frac{f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)}{\omega^{2}},

(587)

where
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
is the spectral
density of the noise coupled to the
\nu
-component of the spin at
site
r
and temperature
T
, and

f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=\begin{cases}f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right),&\text{if }l_{1}-l_{2}=0,\\
f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right),&\text{if }l_{1}-l_{2}\geq 1,\end{cases}

(588)

with

f_{\nu;n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)=2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}}{2}\right),

(589)

f_{\nu;n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}}{2}\right),

(590)

\tilde{w}_{n;l}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{4},&\text{if }l=0,1,2n,2n+1,\\
\frac{1}{2},&\text{if }1<l<2n,\end{cases}

(591)

\sum_{l=a}^{b}F_{l}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if }a>b,\\
F_{a}+F_{a+1}+\ldots+F_{b}&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(592)

and
F_{l}
being an arbitrary sequence. In
spinbosonchain
,
the
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
are represented by

spinbosonchain._influence.eta.Eta
objects. In this appendix,
we discuss in detail how to calculate
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
.

D.1
Caching
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}

Given how computationally expensive it is to evaluate the
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
,
it is beneficial to calculate only the
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}

that are required for a full simulation, cache them, and then reuse
them wherever possible. As discussed briefly in Sec.
III.6
,
there exists an integer
K_{\tau}>0
such that the
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}

for
2n+1\geq l_{1}\geq l_{2}+2K_{\tau}
contribute negligibly to the
dynamics. This is related to the “memory” of the system,
\tau
,
which we informally defined in Sec.
III.6
.

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
can be viewed as a kind of two-time
[or two-point] function where
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
are associated
with the times

t_{1}=\Delta t\sum_{k=0}^{k_{1}}\tilde{w}_{n;l},

(593)

t_{2}=\Delta t\sum_{k=0}^{k_{2}}\tilde{w}_{n;l},

(594)

respectively, with
\tilde{w}_{n;k}
being defined in Eq. (
84
),
and
\Delta t
being the time step size introduced in the QUAPI formalism.
The time difference
t_{1}-t_{2}
is therefore

t_{1}-t_{2}=\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}}\tilde{w}_{n;l}.

(595)

If we let

K_{\tau}=\max\left(0,\left\lceil\frac{\tau-\frac{7}{4}\Delta t}{\Delta t}\right\rceil\right)+3,

(596)

then for all
2n+1\geq l_{1}\geq l_{2}+2K_{\tau}
,
\eta_{\nu;n;l_{1},l_{2}}

will contribute negligibly to the dynamics of the system since
t_{1}-t_{2}\geq\tau
.
The proof is relatively straightforward:

\displaystyle t_{1}-t_{2}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}}\tilde{w}_{n;l}

\displaystyle\geq\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{2}+2K_{\tau}}\tilde{w}_{n;l}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+2K_{\tau}}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+2K_{\tau}-1}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+2}^{l_{2}+2K_{\tau}-2}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+1}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+2K_{\tau}}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+2K_{\tau}-1}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+1}\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}\left(\left[l_{2}+2K_{\tau}-2\right]\right)}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{=\Delta t}\left.\quad\mathord{+}\frac{1}{2}\left(\left[l_{2}+2K_{\tau}-2\right]-\left[l_{2}+2\right]+1\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+2K_{\tau}}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+2K_{\tau}-1}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}+1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2K_{\tau}-3\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;2n+1}+\tilde{w}_{n;2n}+\tilde{w}_{n;1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2K_{\tau}-3\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2K_{\tau}-3\right)\right\}\hphantom{\Delta t\left\{\frac{3}{4}+\left(\left\lceil\frac{\tau-\frac{7}{4}\Delta t}{\Delta t}\right\rceil+\frac{3}{2}\right)\right\}}\hskip 79.6678pt

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\frac{3}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2K_{\tau}-6\right)+\frac{3}{2}\right\}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\frac{7}{4}+\left(K_{\tau}-3\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\frac{7}{4}+\max\left(0,\left\lceil\frac{\tau-\frac{7}{4}\Delta t}{\Delta t}\right\rceil\right)\right\}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\left\{\max\left(\frac{7}{4},\left\lceil\frac{\tau-\frac{7}{4}\Delta t}{\Delta t}\right\rceil+\frac{7}{4}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\tau.

(597)

Thus far we have shown that at most we need only calculate

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
for
2n+1\geq l_{1}\geq l_{2}+2K_{\tau}
.
That being said, as we will demonstrate below, the
f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

[Eq. (
81
)]
that appear in
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
[Eq. (
80
)]
possess certain symmetries that enable us to calculate an even smaller
set of
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
while still capturing essentially
exact dynamics.

Let us simplify
f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

for a variety of different values of
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
. If
l_{1}=l_{2}
,
we have

f_{n;l_{1},l_{1}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right),\quad\text{if }l_{1}\in\left\{0,1,2n,2n+1\right\},

(598)

f_{n;l_{1},l_{1}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right),\quad\text{if }2\leq l_{1}\leq 2n-1,

(599)

If
l_{1}=l_{2}+1=1
, or
l_{1}=l_{2}+1=2n+1
, we have

f_{n;1,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{n;2n+1,2n}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=4e^{-\frac{i}{4}\Delta t\omega}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right),

(600)

If
2n-1\geq l_{1}\geq 2
and
l_{2}=0
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;l_{1},0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{4}+\sum_{l=1}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{4}+\sum_{l=2}^{l_{1}-1}\frac{1}{2}+\tilde{w}_{n;1}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(l_{1}-2\right)+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{3i}{8}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega l_{1}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right),\quad\text{if }2n-1\geq l_{1}\geq 2.

(601)

If
l_{1}=2n
and
l_{2}=0
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;2n,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{l=1}^{2n-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{l=2}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{2}+\tilde{w}_{n;1}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2n-2\right)+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right).

(602)

If
l_{1}=2n+1
and
l_{2}=0
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;2n+1,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{l=1}^{2n}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\tilde{w}_{n;2n}+\sum_{l=2}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{2}+\tilde{w}_{n;1}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2n-2\right)+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{1}{4}-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n+1\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{3i}{4}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n+1\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right).

(603)

If
2n-1\geq l_{1}\geq 2
and
l_{2}=1
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;l_{1},1}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{4}+\sum_{l=2}^{l_{1}-1}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{3}{8}+\frac{1}{2}\left(l_{1}-2\right)\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{i}{8}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{l_{1}-1\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right),\quad\text{if }2n-1\geq l_{1}\geq 2.

(604)

If
l_{1}=2n
and
l_{2}=1
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;2n,1}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{l=2}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2n-2\right)\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{i}{4}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n-1\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{i}{4}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n-1\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right).

(605)

If
l_{1}=2n+1
and
l_{2}=1
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;2n+1,1}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{l=2}^{2n}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\tilde{w}_{n;2n}+\sum_{l=2}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{1}{4}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2n-2\right)+\frac{1}{8}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right).

(606)

If
2n-1\geq l_{1}>l_{2}\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{4}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}-1}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left(l_{1}-l_{2}-1\right)\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{l_{1}-l_{2}\right\}}\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right),\quad\text{if }2n-1\geq l_{1}>l_{2}\geq 2.

(607)

If
l_{1}=2n
, and
2n-1\geq l_{2}\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;2n,l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{3}{8}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2n-l_{2}-1\right)\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{i}{8}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n-l_{2}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right),\quad\text{if }2n-1\geq l_{2}\geq 2.

(608)

If
l_{1}=2n+1
, and
2n-1\geq l_{2}\geq 2
, we have

\displaystyle f_{n;2n+1,l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{2n}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{4}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\tilde{w}_{n;2n}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{8}+\frac{1}{4}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{2n-1}\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{5}{8}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2n-l_{2}-1\right)\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{\frac{3i}{8}\Delta t\omega}e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\omega\left\{2n+1-l_{2}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{4}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega}{8}\right),\quad\text{if }2n-1\geq l_{2}\geq 2.

(609)

From Eqs. (
598
)-(
609
),
we see that the following statements are true [some of which are
trivial]:

f_{n;l_{1},0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}+1,2K_{\tau}+1-l_{1}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }\min\left(2K_{\tau}-1,2n-1\right)\geq l_{1}\geq 0,

(610)

f_{n;2n,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{n;2n,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}-1\geq n\geq 1,

(611)

f_{n;2n+1,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{n;2n+1,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}-1\geq n\geq 1,

(612)

f_{n;l_{1},1}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{K_{\tau}+1;2K_{\tau}+2,2K_{\tau}+3-l_{1}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }\min\left(2K_{\tau},2n-1\right)\geq l_{1}\geq 1,

(613)

f_{n;2n,1}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{n;2n,1}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}\geq n\geq 1,

(614)

f_{n;2n+1,1}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{n;2n,0}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}-1\geq n\geq 1,

(615)

f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{K_{\tau}+2;2K_{\tau}+3,2K_{\tau}+3-l_{1}+l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }2n-1\geq l_{1}\geq l_{2}\geq 2n-2K_{\tau},

(616)

f_{n;2n,l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{K_{\tau}+1;2K_{\tau}+2,2K_{\tau}+2-2n+l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }n\geq K_{\tau}+1,\text{ and }2n\geq l_{2}\geq 2n+1-2K_{\tau},

(617)

and

f_{n;2n+1,l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=f_{K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}+1,2K_{\tau}-2n+l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right),\quad\text{if }n\geq K_{\tau},\text{ and }2n+1\geq l_{2}\geq 2n+2-2K_{\tau}.

(618)

Based on the above results [Eqs. (
610
)-(
618
)],
we define the following cache arrays which we use to calculate the

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
that are required to capture essentially
exact dynamics:

\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};a}^{\left(\text{cache-1}\right)}=\eta_{\nu;r;a+1;2a+2,0},\quad 0\leq a\leq K_{\tau}-2,

(619)

\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};a}^{\left(\text{cache-2}\right)}=\eta_{\nu;r;a+1;2a+3,0},\quad 0\leq a\leq K_{\tau}-2,

(620)

\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};a}^{\left(\text{cache-3}\right)}=\eta_{\nu;r;a+1;2a+2,1},\quad 0\leq a\leq K_{\tau}-1,

(621)

\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};a}^{\left(\text{cache-4}\right)}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau}+2;2K_{\tau}+3,a+4},\quad 0\leq a\leq 2K_{\tau}-1,

(622)

\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};a}^{\left(\text{cache-5}\right)}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau}+1;2K_{\tau}+2,a+3},\quad 0\leq a\leq 2K_{\tau}-1,

(623)

and

\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};a}^{\left(\text{cache-6}\right)}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}+1,a+2},\quad 0\leq a\leq 2K_{\tau}-1.

(624)

The required
\eta_{\nu;r;n;k_{1},k_{2}}
are calculated
using the above cache arrays as follows:

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},0}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}-1-l_{1}}^{\left(\text{cache-6}\right)},\quad\text{if }\min\left(2K_{\tau}-1,2n-1\right)\geq l_{1}\geq 0,

(625)

\eta_{\nu;r;n;2n,0}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};n-1}^{\left(\text{cache-1}\right)},\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}-1\geq n\geq 1,

(626)

\eta_{\nu;r;n;2n+1,0}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};n-1}^{\left(\text{cache-2}\right)},\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}-1\geq n\geq 1,

(627)

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},1}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}-l_{1}}^{\left(\text{cache-5}\right)},\quad\text{if }\min\left(2K_{\tau},2n-1\right)\geq l_{1}\geq 1,

(628)

\eta_{\nu;r;n;2n,1}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};n-1}^{\left(\text{cache-3}\right)},\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}\geq n\geq 1,

(629)

\eta_{\nu;r;n;2n+1,1}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};n-1}^{\left(\text{cache-1}\right)},\quad\text{if }K_{\tau}-1\geq n\geq 1,

(630)

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}-1-l_{1}+l_{2}}^{\left(\text{cache-4}\right)},\quad\text{if }2n-1\geq l_{1}\geq 2,\text{ and }2K_{\tau}-1\geq l_{1}-l_{2},

(631)

\eta_{\nu;r;n;2n,l_{2}}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}-2n-1+l_{2}}^{\left(\text{cache-5}\right)},\quad\text{if }2n\geq l_{2}\geq\max\left(2,2n+1-2K_{\tau}\right),

(632)

and

\eta_{\nu;r;n;2n+1,l_{2}}=\eta_{\nu;r;K_{\tau};2K_{\tau}-2n-2+l_{2}}^{\left(\text{cache-6}\right)},\quad\text{if }2n+1\geq l_{2}\geq\max\left(2,2n+2-2K_{\tau}\right).

(633)

D.2
How to evaluate frequency integrals numerically

The evaluation of
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
[see Eq. (
587
)]
involves computing a frequency integral with an integrand that contains

f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)
which
is highly oscillatory, and as such special care must be taken. Moreover,
the integrand contains a removable singularity at
\omega=0
and
thus requires further special care. In this section, we discuss how
this should be done.

We begin by using

4\sin\left(a\right)\sin\left(b\right)=4\left\{\frac{e^{ia}-e^{-ia}}{2i}\right\}\left\{\frac{e^{ib}-e^{-ib}}{2i}\right\}e^{i\left\{a-b\right\}}+e^{-i\left\{a-b\right\}}-e^{i\left\{a+b\right\}}-e^{-i\left\{a+b\right\}},

(634)

to rewrite
f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)

[Eq. (
81
)]
as follows:

f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)=\begin{cases}f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right),&\text{if }l_{1}-l_{2}=0,\\
f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta k\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right),&\text{if }l_{1}-l_{2}\geq 1,\end{cases}

(635)

\displaystyle f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=2\sin^{2}\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left[1+1-e^{i\Delta t\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}\omega}-e^{-i\Delta t\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}\omega}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\cos\left(\Delta t\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}\omega\right),

(636)

and

\displaystyle f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)

\displaystyle=4e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}}\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}}{2}\right)\sin\left(\frac{\Delta t\omega\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=e^{-i\Delta t\omega\left\{\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}+\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\mathop{\times}\left[e^{\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}-\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}\omega}+e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}-\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}\omega}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{=}\mathop{\times}}\left.\quad\mathop{-}e^{\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}\omega}-e^{-\frac{i}{2}\Delta t\left\{\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}+\tilde{w}_{n;l_{2}}\right\}\omega}\right]

\displaystyle=\left[e^{-i\left\{\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}\right\}\omega}+e^{-i\left\{\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}}\tilde{w}_{n;l}\right\}\omega}\right.

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}\left.\quad\mathop{-}e^{-i\left\{\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l}\right\}\omega}-e^{-i\left\{\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}}^{l_{1}}\tilde{w}_{n;l}\right\}\omega}\right]

\displaystyle=e^{-iW_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\omega}+e^{-iW_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\omega}-e^{-iW_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\omega}-e^{-iW_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\omega},

(637)

with

\displaystyle W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l},

(638)

\displaystyle W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}}\tilde{w}_{n;l},

(639)

\displaystyle W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}+1}^{l_{1}-1}\tilde{w}_{n;l},

(640)

\displaystyle W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}

\displaystyle=\Delta t\sum_{l=l_{2}}^{l_{1}}\tilde{w}_{n;l}.

(641)

Next, we decompose
f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)

and
f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)

into their real and imaginary parts:

f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)=\text{Re}\left[f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]+i\text{Im}\left[f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right],

(642)

with

\text{Re}\left[f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]=1-\cos\left(\left\{\Delta t\tilde{w}_{n;l_{1}}\right\}\omega\right),

(643)

and

\text{Im}\left[f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]=0;

(644)

and

f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)=\text{Re}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]+i\text{Im}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right],

(645)

with

\text{Re}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]=\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\omega\right)+\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\omega\right)-\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\omega\right)-\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\omega\right),

(646)

and

\text{Im}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]=-\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\omega\right)-\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\omega\right)+\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\omega\right)+\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\omega\right).

(647)

Note that we can rewrite Eq. (
643
)
as

\text{Re}\left[f_{n;l_{1}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l=0\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]=\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\omega\right)+\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\omega\right)-\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\omega\right)-\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\omega\right),

(648)

using the summing convention of Eq. (
592
)
such that the right-hand-side is effectively identical in form to
that of Eq. (
646
).

As mentioned at the beginning of this section, the integrand of the
frequency integral that is computed in order to evaluate
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}

has a removable singularity at
\omega=0
[see Eqs. (
80
)-(
38
)].
As a result, special care needs to be taken in evaluating the integrand
in the neighbourhood of
\omega=0
. First, we Taylor expand
\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\text{Re}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]

[using Eqs. (
646
)
and (
648
) in
the process]:

\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\text{Re}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{m}\omega^{2m-2}}{\left(2m\right)!}\left[\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\right\}^{2m}+\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\right\}^{2m}-\left\{W_{\nu n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\right\}^{2m}-\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\right\}^{2m}\right].

(649)

Next, we Taylor expand
\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\text{Im}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]

[using Eq. (
647
)
in the process]:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\text{Im}\left[f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,\Delta l\geq 1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right]

\displaystyle=-\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{m}\left(\Delta t\right)^{2m+1}\left(\omega\right)^{2m-1}}{\left(2m+1\right)!}\left[\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\right\}^{2m+1}+\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\right\}^{2m+1}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{=}-\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{m}\left(\Delta t\right)^{2m+1}\left(\omega\right)^{2m-1}}{\left(2m+1\right)!}}\left.\quad\mathop{-}\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\right\}^{2m+1}-\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\right\}^{2m+1}\right].

(650)

In practice, we perform both Taylor expansions up to
m=3

in the neighbourhood
\left|\omega\right|<10^{-3}/W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}

where

W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}=\max\left(\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(j\right)}\right\}_{u=0}^{3}\right),

(651)

otherwise we use Eqs. (
644
)
and (
646
)-(
648
).

From Eq. (
653
), we
see that one must evaluate
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
–
which is the spectral density of the noise coupled to the
\nu
-component
of the spin at site
r
and temperature
T
– in order
to evaluate
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
. As discussed in Sec.
III.4
,

A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
will comprise of multiple components:

A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)=\sum_{\varsigma}A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right),

(652)

When
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)
contains multiple
components as described above, we can express
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}

as

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}=\sum_{\varsigma}\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma},

(653)

where

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\,A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)\frac{f_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta\right)}\left(\omega\right)}{\omega^{2}}.

(654)

In practice, it is best to evaluate each
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma}

according to Eq. (
654
)
and then add them all together according to Eq. (
653
)
to get
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
.

Next, using Eqs. (
642
)-(
651
),
we partition the integral in Eq. (
654
)
as:

\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma}\approx\sum_{a=0}^{8}\left\{\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}+i\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}\right\},

(655)

where

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{2,3,5,6\right\}}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}=\int_{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a}}^{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a+1}}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\,A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)F_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}\left(\omega\right),

(656)

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{2,3,5,6\right\}}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}=\int_{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a}}^{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a+1}}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\,A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)F_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}\left(\omega\right),

(657)

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{0,1,4,7,8\right\}}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}=\sum_{j=0}^{3}\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a;j}^{\left(\eta,1\right)},

(658)

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{0,1,4,7,8\right\}}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}=\sum_{j=0}^{3}\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a;j}^{\left(\eta,2\right)},

(659)

with

\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=0}=-\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)},

(660)

\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=2}=\min\left(\max\left(-\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)},\omega^{\left(A\right)}\right),-\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)}\right),

(661)

\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=4}=-\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)},

(662)

\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=1}=\begin{cases}\omega^{\left(B\right)},&\text{if }\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=0}<\omega^{\left(B\right)}<\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=2},\\
\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=0},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases},

(663)

\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=3}=\begin{cases}\omega^{\left(B\right)},&\text{if }\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=2}<\omega^{\left(B\right)}<\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=4},\\
\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a=4},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases},

(664)

\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a}=-\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;9-a},\quad\text{if }a\geq 5.

(665)

\omega^{\left(A\right)}=\begin{cases}-\frac{\pi}{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}},&\text{if }W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}\neq 0,\\
-\infty,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(666)

\omega^{\left(B\right)}=-\frac{25}{\beta},

(667)

\beta=1/\left(k_{B}T\right)
being the inverse temperature,

\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{IR}\right)}
and
\omega_{\nu;\varsigma}^{\left(\text{UV}\right)}

being the hard infrared and ultraviolet cutoff frequencies of
A_{\nu;r;T}\left(\omega\right)

[see Eqs. (
584
)-(
586
)],

\displaystyle\left.F_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right|_{\left|\omega\right|<10^{-3}/W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{3}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{m}\omega^{2m-2}}{\left(2m\right)!}\left[\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\right\}^{2m}+\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\right\}^{2m}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{=}\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{m}\omega^{2m-2}}{\left(2m\right)!}}\left.\quad\mathop{-}\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\right\}^{2m}-\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\right\}^{2m}\right],

(668)

\displaystyle\left.F_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right|_{\left|\omega\right|\geq 10^{-3}/W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}\left[\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\omega\right)+\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\omega\right)\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{=}\frac{1}{\omega^{2}}}\left.\quad\mathop{-}\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\omega\right)-\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\omega\right)\right],

(669)

\displaystyle\left.F_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right|_{\left|\omega\right|<10^{-3}/W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}}

\displaystyle=-\left(1-\delta_{l_{1},l_{2}}\right)\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{m}\left(\omega\right)^{2m-1}}{\left(2m+1\right)!}\left[\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\right\}^{2m+1}+\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\right\}^{2m+1}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{=}-\left(1-\delta_{l_{1},l_{2}}\right)\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-1\right)^{m}\left(\omega\right)^{2m-1}}{\left(2m+1\right)!}}\left.\quad\mathop{-}\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\right\}-\left\{W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\right\}^{2m+1}\right],

(670)

\displaystyle\left.F_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}\left(\omega\right)\right|_{\left|\omega\right|\geq 10^{-3}/W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(\max\right)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(1-\delta_{l_{1},l_{2}}\right)}{\omega^{2}}\left[-\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(0\right)}\omega\right)-\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(1\right)}\omega\right)\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{\mathrel{=}\frac{\left(1-\delta_{l_{1},l_{2}}\right)}{\omega^{2}}}\left.\quad\mathop{+}\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}\omega\right)+\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(3\right)}\omega\right)\right],

(671)

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{0,1,4,7,8\right\};j}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}=\left(-1\right)^{\left\lfloor j/2\right\rfloor}\int_{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a}}^{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a+1}}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\,\frac{A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)}{\omega^{2}}\cos\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(j\right)}\omega\right),

(672)

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{0,1,4,7,8\right\};j}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}=-\left(-1\right)^{\left\lfloor j/2\right\rfloor}\left(1-\delta_{l_{1},l_{2}}\right)\int_{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a}}^{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a+1}}\frac{d\omega}{2\pi}\,\frac{A_{\nu;r;T;\varsigma}\left(\omega\right)}{\omega^{2}}\sin\left(W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(j\right)}\omega\right),

(673)

In practice, it is best to evaluate each
\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{2,3,5,6\right\}}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}
,

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{2,3,5,6\right\}}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}
,

\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{0,1,4,7,8\right\};j}^{\left(\eta,1\right)}
,
and
\mathcal{I}_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2};\varsigma;a\in\left\{0,1,4,7,8\right\};j}^{\left(\eta,2\right)}

according to Eqs. (
656
)-(
673
),
and then add them all together according to Eqs. (
653
)
and (
655
) to get
\eta_{\nu;r;n;l_{1},l_{2}}
.

In
spinbosonchain
, the integrals in Eqs. (
656
)
and (
657
)
are evaluated using
Python
’s

scipy.integrate.quad
’s default adaptive integrator since
the corresponding integrands in these cases are not highly oscillatory,
considering the limits of integrations. An upper bound of
2000

subintervals is used in the integrator algorithm, which should yield
a negligible integration error compared to the other sources of error
in our QUAPI approach [e.g. the Trotterization error]. When
W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(j\right)}=0
,
the integral in Eq. (
673
)
evaluates to zero hence no explicit numerical integration is required,
whereas the integral in Eq. (
672
)
can be evaluated using
scipy.integrate.quad
’s default
adaptive integrator with an upper bound of
2000
subintervals used
in the integrator algorithm, since the integrand is not highly oscillatory
– if at all. When
W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(j\right)}>0
,
the integrands in Eqs. (
672
)
and (
673
)
are highly oscillatory. Because of this, the integral in Eq. (
672
)
is evaluated using
scipy.integrate.quad
with the keyword
arguments
weight=‘cos’
and
\texttt{wvar=}W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(j\right)}
,
and in Eq. (
673
)
the integral is evaluated with the keyword arguments
weight=‘sin’

and
\texttt{wvar=}W_{n;l_{1},l_{2}}^{\left(j\right)}
. In both cases,
the upper bound of subintervals used are proportional to the number
of completed oscillations across the range of integration. In
spinbosonchain

we set the upper bound of subintervals to:

\texttt{limit}=2000\left\lfloor\frac{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a+1}-\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a}}{\omega_{\nu;r;n;l_{1};l_{2};\varsigma;a}}+1\right\rfloor,

(674)

where
limit
refers to the keyword argument in

scipy.integrate.quad
that specifies the upper bound of
subintervals used.

Appendix E
MPS compression techniques used in
spinbosonchain

As discussed in Sec.
IV.4
,

spinbosonchain
represents the local path influence functionals

\mathring{I}_{r;n}\left(\cdots\right)
[Eq. (
103
)]
by a set of MPS’s. Similarly, the quantities
\mathring{\rho}_{m}^{\left(\vdash\right)}\left(\mathbf{b}_{m}\right)
,

\mathring{\rho}_{n;m}\left(\mathbf{b}_{m}\right)
[introduced
in Sec.
IV.5
]
and the system’s reduced density matrix
\mathring{\rho}_{n}^{\left(A\right)}\left(\mathbf{j}_{\left(n+1\right)\Delta m}\right)

are also represented by MPS’s. Each of the aforementioned MPS’s are
updated by applying to it a MPO, which increases subsequently the
bond dimensions, thus increasing memory requirements and the simulation
runtime. To address this,
spinbosonchain
offers a variety
of MPS compression methods that can be used to improve the computationally
efficiency of the simulations.

For finite MPS’s, users can apply either the “zip-up” method
(
Stoudenmire and White 2010
)

or the “direct” method
(
Stoudenmire and White 2010
)
, the latter consists
of performing a singular value decomposition (SVD) or QR sweep in
one direct without truncating the Schmidt spectra, followed by a SVD
sweep in the opposite direction with truncation. By truncating the
Schmidt spectra, one truncates the bond dimensions. Of course, such
an operation introduces a truncation error, however this can be controlled
to some degree. For a detailed discussion on SVD truncation sweeps,
see e.g. Ref.
(
Schollwöck 2011
)
. For detailed discussions on the
“zip-up” method, see e.g. Refs.
(
Paeckel et al. 2019
;
Stoudenmire and White 2010
)
.

One can improve the compression of a finite MPS by performing a subsequent
set of variational compression sweeps. This may be worthwhile for
a MPS representing a local path influence functional, however it is
likely to be too costly for a MPS that spans space (e.g. one representing
the system state) rather than time (e.g. a local path influence functional).
It is recommended to consider only this feature for MPS’s spanning
time. Moreover, this variational compression feature is not available
for infinite MPS’s, which are MPS’s with infinitely many unit cells.

For infinite MPS’s with single site unit cells,
spinbosonchain

employs the compression method described in Ref.
(
Orús and Vidal 2008
)
.
For infinite MPS’s with multi-site unit cells,
spinbosonchain

employs the compression method described in Ref.
(
Orús and Vidal 2008
;
McCulloch 2008
)
.

In
spinbosonchain
, users specify MPS compression related
parameters using the

spinbosonchain.compress.Params
class. Basic SVD and QR
related operations are found in the modules

spinbosonchain._svd
and
spinbosonchain._qr

respectively. All compression methods in
spinbosonchain

are implemented in the
spinbosonchain._mpomps
module.
This module also includes implementations for MPO applications to
MPS’s.
